It uses\n4: procedural shapes instead of sprites and keeps game rules in the shared `core`\n5: library so the terminal playtest, graphical client, and backend use the same\n6: logic.\n7: \n8: ## Requirements\n9: \n10: - C++20 compiler\n11: - CMake 3.25+\n12: - raylib\n13: - OpenSSL\n14: - Docker Desktop or another Docker Engine for the production API stack\n15: \n16: On macOS with Homebrew:\n17: \n18: ```bash\n19: brew install cmake raylib openssl\n20: ```\n21: \n22: ## Build and test locally\n23: \n24: ```bash\n25: cmake -S . -B build -DCMAKE_PREFIX_PATH=/opt/homebrew\n26: cmake --build build -j 4\n27: ctest --test-dir build --output-on-failure\n28: ```\n29: \n30: Run the graphical client:\n31: \n32: ```bash\n33: ./build/client/emberdeep_graphical\n34: ```\n35: \n36: Run the terminal playtest:\n37: \n38: ```bash\n39: ./build/client/emberdeep_playtest\n40: ```\n41: \n42: ## Equipment gems\n43: \n44: Gear from tier 4 onward can have sockets. Gems found during dungeon runs or\n45: other progression can be inserted into the selected gear piece from the\n46: Inventory screen:\n47: \n48: - `G` inserts the first available gem into the selected gear piece.\n49: - `R` removes the selected piece's last socketed gem and returns it to inventory.\n50: \n51: The Inventory screen shows each piece's used and available sockets. Socketed\n52: gems are saved with the equipment, and their bonuses apply while that piece is\n53: equipped. Gem definitions and their effects are listed in\n54: `core/include/ed/data_model.hpp`.\n55: \n56: ## Backend deployment\n57: \n58: The production profile uses PostgreSQL 16, the C++ API, and Caddy for HTTPS.\n59: \n60: ```bash\n61: cp .env.example .env\n62: # Edit .env and replace every placeholder with a strong secret.\n63: docker compose up -d --build --pull always\n64: ```\n65: \n66: Check the services:\n67: \n68: ```bash\n69: curl https://your-domain.example/healthz\n70: curl https://your-domain.example/readyz\n71: docker compose ps\n72: docker compose logs -f api\n73: ```\n74: \n75: For local client playtesting while the containers are running, the API is\n76: available at `http://127.0.0.1:8080`.\n77: \n78: Existing database volumes created before the split PostgreSQL roles need the\n79: one-time upgrade script documented in `deploy/README.md`.\n80: \n81: PostgreSQL data lives in the `postgres_data` volume, and API fallback state lives\n82: in `api_data`, so recreating containers does not remove saved data. Authenticated\n83: sessions default to 30 days (`EMBERDEEP_ACCESS_TOKEN_TTL_SECONDS`); migration\n84: `0003` extends existing active sessions once so a server relaunch does not\n85: invalidate the token stored by the desktop client.\n86: \n87: ## Accounts and local saves\n88: \n89: Guest progress is stored locally. Signed-in progress is stored locally and\n90: synchronized to the backend. Local saves are username-bound:\n91: \n92: ```text\n93: emberdeep-guest.sav\n94: emberdeep-<username>.sav\n95: ```\n96: \n97: Signing out does not delete any save. The next account can sign in or play as a\n98: guest, and switching back loads that account's local save before cloud sync.\n99: The account profile is stored in `emberdeep.profile` and is ignored by Git.\n100: \n101: ## Inspecting PostgreSQL data\n102: \n103: Open a `psql` shell as the administrator:\n104: \n105: ```bash\n106: set -a\n107: . .env\n108: set +a\n109: DB=${POSTGRES_DB:-emberdeep}\n110: docker compose exec -T \\\n111:   -e PGPASSWORD=\"$POSTGRES_ADMIN_PASSWORD\" \\\n112:   db psql -h 127.0.0.1 \\\n113:   -U \"$POSTGRES_ADMIN_USER\" \\\n114:   -d \"$DB\"\n115: ```\n116: \n117: Useful queries:\n118: \n119: ```sql\n120: \\dt\n121: \\du\n122: SELECT id, public_id, username, status, created_at, last_seen_at\n123: FROM accounts\n124: ORDER BY id;\n125: \n126: SELECT account_id, revision, schema_version, progress_score, created_at\n127: FROM saves\n128: ORDER BY account_id, revision DESC;\n129: \n130: SELECT count(*) AS account_count FROM accounts;\n131: SELECT count(*) AS save_revision_count FROM saves;\n132: \\q\n133: ```\n134: \n135: One-off commands without opening a shell:\n136: \n137: ```bash\n138: docker compose exec -T \\\n139:   -e PGPASSWORD=\"$POSTGRES_ADMIN_PASSWORD\" \\\n140:   db psql -h 127.0.0.1 -U \"$POSTGRES_ADMIN_USER\" -d \"$DB\" \\\n141:   -c 'SELECT username, status, created_at FROM accounts ORDER BY id;'\n142: ```\n143: \n144: Back up the database:\n145: \n146: ```bash\n147: docker compose exec -T \\\n148:   -e PGPASSWORD=\"$POSTGRES_ADMIN_PASSWORD\" \\\n149:   db pg_dump -h 127.0.0.1 -U \"$POSTGRES_ADMIN_USER\" \"$DB\" \\\n150:   > \"emberdeep-$(date +%F).sql\"\n151: ```\n152: \n153: ## Repository layout\n154: \n155: | Path | Purpose |\n156: |---|---|\n157: | `core/` | Shared game rules, data models, saves, stats, and cloud client |\n158: | `client/` | Graphical and terminal clients |\n159: | `server/` | API, PostgreSQL backend, migrations, and database scripts |\n160: | `tests/` | Catch2 core and persistence tests |\n161: | `data/` | Data-driven materials, layers, bosses, and dungeon definitions |\n162: | `deploy/` | Caddy and deployment documentation |\n163: \n164: Never commit `.env`, database dumps, local save files, profile files, or\n165: generated build output.\n166: \nThe above content shows the entire, complete file contents of the requested file.\n"}

Please note that any changes targeting the original code should remove the line number, colon, and leading space.\n1: # Emberdeep: The Hollow Crown — Game Design & Technical Spec\n2: \n3: > **Working title.** A medieval-magical clicker game about mining, crafting, and conquering a 10-boss campaign.\n4: > **Audience for this document:** a coding agent building the game in TypeScript + React.\n5: > **All numbers are starting values.** Put them in data files so they can be tuned without touching logic.\n6: \n7: ---\n8: \n9: # Emberdeep: The Hollow Crown — Game Design & Technical Spec (v2)\n10: \n11: > **Working title.** A medieval-magical clicker about mining, crafting and conquering a 10-boss campaign.\n12: > **What changed in v2:** native **C++20 client** (replaces TypeScript/React); a **self-hosted C++ backend + PostgreSQL** for cloud saves; **guilds, an async PvP arena and leaderboards**; **Docker** for the server stack; and an **optional free-asset acquisition pipeline**. Sections 1 and 3–13 (game design) are unchanged except where they touch platform, controls or online features.\n13: > **Audience:** a coding agent. All numbers are starting values and live in data files.\n14: \n15: ---\n16: \n17: ## 0. Instructions for the Coding Agent\n18: \n19: 1. Build in the **phases in Section 24**. Finish and verify one phase before starting the next.\n20: 2. **Single source of truth for game data:** JSON files in `/data` (materials, gear, gems, recipes, enemies, bosses, perks, layers, arena, guild). Both the client and the server load and validate the same files. Game rules live in the shared **`core`** library (no rendering, no networking, no file I/O).\n21: 3. **Assets are optional.** Everything must render and make sound with **zero external assets** using procedural shapes and synthesized audio (the permanent fallback). Downloaded assets (Section 21) are an enhancement layered on top and must follow the licensing rules there.\n22: 4. **C++20, CMake, vcpkg (manifest mode, pinned baseline).** Compile with warnings as errors (`-Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic` / `/W4 /WX`), run `clang-format` and `clang-tidy`, and use ASan/UBSan in debug CI. Tests use **Catch2 v3**.\n23: 5. **Determinism:** gameplay randomness uses the shared **PCG32** RNG in `core`. **Do not** use `std::uniform_*_distribution` (its output differs between standard libraries). Combat uses **integer / basis-point math only**, so the client and server always agree.\n24: 6. **Never block the render thread.** Disk and network I/O run on worker threads and hand results back through a thread-safe queue. Never share mutable game state across threads; pass immutable snapshots.\n25: 7. **Desktop-first:** Windows, Linux, macOS. Logical resolution 1280×720 (16:9), scaled to the window with letterboxing. Mouse + keyboard; gamepad is optional and comes late.\n26: 8. **The game is offline-first.** Solo play never requires the server. Online features degrade gracefully (clear \"Offline\" state, no crashes, no lost progress).\n27: 9. When something is ambiguous, pick the simplest option, record it in `docs/DECISIONS.md`, and continue.\n28: \n29: ---\n30: ---\n31: ## 1. Game Pillars\n32: \n33: - **Satisfying clicks.** Every hit needs punch: shake, particles, floating numbers, cracks.\n34: - **Mine → Craft → Conquer.** Mining feeds crafting; crafting feeds power; power (plus skill) beats bosses.\n35: - **Skill checks, not just stats.** Campaign bosses are short mini-games (Undertale/Punch-Out/osu!mania flavor). Gear helps but never replaces skill.\n36: - **Long tail.** After beating all 10 bosses, Echo Ranks, Deep Strata, an endless Abyss dungeon and Ascension keep progression going.\n37: \n38: ---\n39: \n40: ## 2. Tech Stack & Architecture\n41: \n42: ### 2.1 Technology Choices\n43: \n44: **Client**\n45: \n46: | Concern | Choice |\n47: |---|---|\n48: | Language / build | C++20 (GCC 13+, Clang 17+, MSVC 2022), CMake ≥ 3.25 with `CMakePresets.json`, vcpkg manifest mode |\n49: | Window, 2D graphics, input, audio | **raylib** (latest stable, pin the exact version). Covers OpenGL-backed 2D shapes/polygons/gradients, textures, fonts, shaders, render textures, gamepad, and built-in audio (streamed OGG music, `Sound` SFX, procedural `Wave`/`AudioStream` synthesis) |\n50: | GUI | A **custom retained-mode UI layer** in `client/src/ui/`: `Panel, Button, Label, ProgressBar, Tabs, ScrollList, ItemGrid, Tooltip, Modal, TextInput, Toast`, driven by a `theme.json`. `raygui` is allowed for dev/debug tools only. Reason: this game needs a themed stone/parchment look, and stock immediate-mode widgets look like tools |\n51: | HTTP / WebSocket | `cpr` (libcurl) for REST, `IXWebSocket` for WSS |\n52: | JSON, compression | `nlohmann/json`, `zstd` |\n53: | Crypto | `libsodium` (hashing, random, HMAC); TLS via libcurl/OpenSSL |\n54: | Logging | `spdlog` |\n55: | Tests | Catch2 v3 |\n56: \n57: **Server**\n58: \n59: | Concern | Choice |\n60: |---|---|\n61: | Framework | **Drogon** (async C++ HTTP + WebSocket, built-in PostgreSQL client) |\n62: | Database | **PostgreSQL 16**, self-hosted, raw SQL migrations |\n63: | Cache / rate limits | Optional **Redis 7** (Compose profile `scale`); in-memory fallback for a single instance |\n64: | Shared code | Links the same `core` library and `/data` files as the client, so the server can recompute stats and re-run combat itself |\n65: | Packaging | Docker multi-stage image (Section 20) |\n66: \n67: **Why raylib:** one dependency covers window, drawing primitives, audio and input; its shape-drawing API (`DrawPoly`, `DrawRectangleRounded`, `DrawCircleGradient`, `DrawSplineBezier`, `DrawRing`) matches this game's \"art made from shapes\" approach; and it builds cleanly with CMake/vcpkg. **Alternatives** if raylib blocks the project: SDL3 + SDL_ttf + SDL_mixer, or SFML 3 + TGUI. To keep that swap possible, screens and minigames must draw through a thin `gfx::` wrapper and play sound through `audio::`, never through raw raylib calls scattered around. Record any switch in `DECISIONS.md`.\n68: \n69: **Why a C++ server:** the combat simulator, stat calculator and validation rules are shared code, so PvP results and plausibility checks are computed by exactly the same logic the client uses. That also removes a whole class of \"client and server disagree\" bugs.\n70: \n71: ### 2.2 Repository Layout\n72: ```\n73: emberdeep/\n74:   CMakeLists.txt  CMakePresets.json  vcpkg.json\n75:   core/                    # shared static lib (client + server + tools + tests)\n76:     include/ed/  src/      #   rng, fixed math, formulas, stats, mining, crafting, combat,\n77:                            #   progression, gemevent, echo, ascension, save schema, validation, api DTOs\n78:   data/                    # JSON game data (validated against schemas in data/schema/)\n79:   client/src/\n80:     app/ screens/ ui/ gfx/ audio/ minigames/ net/ save/ assets/\n81:   server/\n82:     src/  api/ (controllers)  services/  db/  ws/  jobs/\n83:     migrations/*.sql\n84:   tools/                   # asset fetcher (Python), balance simulator, chart tools, admin CLI\n85:   assets/                  # optional downloaded assets + manifest.json (Section 21)\n86:   docker/                  # Dockerfile, compose files, Caddyfile, backup scripts\n87:   tests/                   # core unit tests, server integration tests\n88:   docs/DECISIONS.md\n89: ```\n90: \n91: ### 2.3 Client Architecture\n92: - **Main loop:** fixed **60 Hz update**, rendering every frame with interpolation where useful. Everything is drawn into a 1280×720 render texture, then scaled to the window.\n93: - **Screens:** a stack (`Main, Inventory, Crafting, Profile, Campaign, Minigame, Dungeon, GemHollow, Guild, Arena, Leaderboards, Settings`). Modals sit above the stack.\n94: - **Services** (owned by `App`, passed by reference to screens): `SaveService` (local), `SyncService` (cloud, worker thread), `NetClient`, `AudioService`, `AssetManager` (falls back to procedural drawing), `TimeService`, `Settings`.\n95: - **State:** plain structs in `core` (`GameState`), mutated only on the main thread by `core` system functions. UI reads state; UI never contains rules.\n96: - **Events:** a small typed event bus (`BlockBroken`, `LevelUp`, `BossCleared`, `ItemCrafted`, …) drives toasts, achievements, sound, and \"mark save dirty\".\n97: \n98: ### 2.4 Core Rules\n99: - **Number formatting:** `1.2K, 3.4M, 5.6B, …` via `core::format`. Currencies are `double`; combat values are `int64_t`.\n100: - **Time:** `TimeService` returns server-corrected UTC (offset learned from `GET /v1/time`), falling back to the local clock offline. Daily and weekly resets use it.\n101: - **Local save:** JSON → zstd → `.sav` in the OS user-data directory (`%APPDATA%`, `~/.local/share`, `~/Library/Application Support`). Writes are **atomic** (temp file + rename), with **3 rolling backups**. Includes `schema_version` and migrations. Export/Import via file and a base64 string.\n102: - **Pause:** minigames pause on window focus loss.\n103: \n104: \n105: \n106: \n107: \n108: ## 3. Main Screen\n109: \n110: ### Layout (portrait; landscape stretches horizontally)\n111: ```\n112: ┌────────────────────────────────────────┐\n113: │ ☆ Lv 12  [XP ▓▓▓▓░░]        🪙 1,240   │  top bar: level, xp bar, gold\n114: ├────────────────────────────────────────┤\n115: │         Layer: Iron Hollows ▾          │  layer selector\n116: │                                        │\n117: │        ▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓░░░░  620/900         │  block health bar (above block)\n118: │        ╭── Rare Iron Ore ──╮           │  name + rarity tag\n119: │        │      ⬢ BLOCK ⬢     │          │  big clickable SVG block\n120: │        ╰────────────────────╯          │\n121: │   HP 900 · Hardness 10 · XP 29         │  block stat chips\n122: │   Rarity: Rare (×2.5 HP, ×3 drops)     │\n123: │                                        │\n124: │ [ 💎 Gem Hollow – READY ]              │  daily event button (glows when available)\n125: ├────────────────────────────────────────┤\n126: │ [Inventory] [Crafting] [Dungeon]       │\n127: │ [Campaign ] [Profile ]                 │  bottom navigation\n128: └────────────────────────────────────────┘\n129: ```\n130: \n131: ### Required UI Elements\n132: - **Block health bar** above the block. Numeric HP text, smooth drain, flashes on crit.\n133: - **Block info:** material, rarity tag, max HP, hardness (\"strength\"), XP given, and an expected-drops tooltip.\n134: - **Layer selector:** choose which layer's blocks spawn (unlocked layers only).\n135: - **Equipped pickaxe** shown as a shape-built pickaxe that swings on each click.\n136: - **Bottom nav:** Inventory, Profile, Crafting, Campaign, plus Dungeon. Icons are SVG primitives.\n137: - **Gem Hollow button:** greyed with a countdown (\"Resets in 06:12:40\") when used today; golden glow when ready.\n138: - **Toasts** for level-ups, rare drops, unlocks.\n139: \n140: ### Feedback / Juice (all shape-based)\n141: - Block squash on hit, screen shake scaled to crit.\n142: - Crack overlay: SVG paths revealed at 75%, 50%, 25% HP.\n143: - Particles: small polygons in the material's color, gravity and fade.\n144: - Floating damage numbers (white normal, gold crit, larger on crit).\n145: - Break: block shatters into ~12 shard polygons; drops fly to a \"loot\" indicator.\n146: - **Weak Spot (optional but recommended):** a glowing rune circle appears on the block every 4–8 s. Clicking it is a guaranteed crit for +50% damage. It disappears after 1.5 s.\n147: \n148: ---\n149: \n150: ## 4. Mining System\n151: \n152: ### 4.1 Damage Formula\n153: ```\n154: rawDamage        = miningPower × (1 + powerBonus%) × (isCrit ? critDamage : 1)\n155: effectiveHardness = max(0, block.hardness − pierce)\n156: finalDamage      = rawDamage × 100 / (100 + effectiveHardness)   // min 1\n157: ```\n158: - `critChance` base 5%, `critDamage` base ×1.5.\n159: - Holding a click/touch does **not** auto-repeat by default (true clicker). Optional setting \"Hold to mine\" at 5 hits/s.\n160: - Rapid-fire limit: max 15 registered hits/s to prevent autoclicker abuse from breaking balance.\n161: \n162: ### 4.2 Block Generation\n163: When a block breaks, the next spawns:\n164: 1. Pick a **material tier** from the selected layer: **75%** layer tier `n`, **20%** tier `n−1` (if n>1), **5%** tier `n+1` (bonus tough block, if it exists).\n165: 2. Roll a **rarity** (Luck stat shifts weight toward higher rarities).\n166: 3. Final HP, XP and drops = material base × rarity multiplier.\n167: \n168: ### 4.3 Block Rarity\n169: | Rarity | Base Weight | HP × | XP × | Drop Qty × | Extras |\n170: |---|---|---|---|---|---|\n171: | Common | 70% | 1.0 | 1.0 | 1.0 | — |\n172: | Uncommon | 22% | 1.5 | 1.5 | 2.0 | Faint sparkle |\n173: | Rare | 6.5% | 2.5 | 3.0 | 3.0 | +Arcane Dust chance ×2, glowing outline |\n174: | Epic | 1.5% | 5.0 | 5.0 | 6.0 | Guaranteed Arcane Dust, animated aura |\n175: \n176: Luck: each point of Luck moves 0.1% weight from Common into the higher rarities proportionally (cap +15%).\n177: \n178: ### 4.4 Drops\n179: - **Ore:** 1–2 of the block's material × rarity qty multiplier.\n180: - **Arcane Dust:** 8% base chance per block (any material), 1–3 dust. Used in upgrades and enchanting.\n181: - **Gold:** none directly from blocks. Gold comes from selling and the dungeon.\n182: - **Double drop:** Luck gives `Luck × 0.2%` chance to double ore.\n183: \n184: ---\n185: \n186: ## 5. Materials & Layers (Progression Ladder)\n187: \n188: Ten tiers, one per layer. Layer *n* is unlocked when **player level ≥ layer.minLevel** and you own a **pickaxe of tier ≥ n−1**. Layer 1 is open from the start.\n189: \n190: | # | Material | Layer | Color (hex) | Block Look (shapes) | HP | Hardness | XP | Sell (ore) | Min Lv |\n191: |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n192: | 1 | **Ashen Stone** | Ashen Warrens | `#8a8a94` | Grey rounded boulder, pale facets | 20 | 0 | 5 | 1 | 1 |\n193: | 2 | **Copper** | Copper Crypt | `#c9773f` | Orange-brown hex with green verdigris specks | 52 | 4 | 12 | 3 | 5 |\n194: | 3 | **Iron** | Iron Hollows | `#7b8794` | Blue-grey block, riveted corners | 135 | 10 | 29 | 8 | 10 |\n195: | 4 | **Silvervein** | Silvermire | `#d7e2ec` | Pale block with bright zig-zag veins | 350 | 20 | 69 | 20 | 15 |\n196: | 5 | **Aurelite** | Aurelite Keep | `#f2c14e` | Gold-yellow crystals, warm glow | 915 | 36 | 166 | 50 | 20 |\n197: | 6 | **Runic Slate** | Runic Archives | `#5a4fcf` | Violet slab with pulsing glowing rune | 2,380 | 60 | 398 | 125 | 27 |\n198: | 7 | **Starmetal** | Stargazer Spire | `#6fd3ff` | Dark navy meteor chunk with twinkling star points | 6,190 | 95 | 955 | 310 | 34 |\n199: | 8 | **Wyrmite** | Wyrmbone Caverns | `#d94f30` | Bone-white and ember-red striped shard | 16,100 | 145 | 2,290 | 780 | 42 |\n200: | 9 | **Voidglass** | Voidglass Sanctum | `#2b0f3a` | Near-black glassy prism with magenta edge light | 41,800 | 210 | 5,500 | 1,950 | 50 |\n201: | 10 | **Aetherium** | The Aether Vault | `#7cf7e0` | Iridescent cyan/pink shifting crystal | 108,700 | 300 | 13,200 | 4,900 | 60 |\n202: \n203: Formulas behind the table (use them to generate later tiers): `HP = 20 × 2.6^(n−1)`, `XP = 5 × 2.4^(n−1)`, `sell = 1 × 2.5^(n−1)`.\n204: \n205: ### Deep Strata (post-game, endless)\n206: After beating the final boss, layer 10 gains a **Depth** counter. Depth `d ≥ 1` generates blocks with `HP × 2.6^d`, `XP × 2.4^d`, ore drops of Aetherium ×(1 + 0.5d), and a growing chance of **Aether Shards** (used in Mythic upgrades, Section 8.4).\n207: \n208: ### Secondary Materials\n209: | Item | Source | Use |\n210: |---|---|---|\n211: | **Arcane Dust** | Any block (8%) | Upgrades, gem cutting |\n212: | **Ingots** (one per ore) | Smelting | Gear crafting |\n213: | **Trophies** (one per dungeon zone) | Dungeon enemies | Gear crafting (Section 9) |\n214: | **Boss Essence** | Boss first clears and star rewards | Legendary Awakening |\n215: | **Echo Sigils** | Echo bosses (post-game) | Mythic upgrades |\n216: | **Aether Shards** | Deep Strata | Mythic upgrades |\n217: | **Ember Marks** | Ascension | Permanent perks |\n218: \n219: ---\n220: \n221: ## 6. Gemstones & Daily Event\n222: \n223: Gems are **not** found in normal blocks. They come from the **Gem Hollow** daily event, boss star rewards and rare dungeon chests.\n224: \n225: ### 6.1 Gem Table\n226: | Rarity | Weight | Gems | Effect when socketed (Rough / Cut / Flawless = ×1 / ×2 / ×4) |\n227: |---|---|---|---|\n228: | Common | 55% | **Amber** | +1 Luck |\n229: | | | **Garnet** | +2% Attack |\n230: | Uncommon | 28% | **Jade** | +2% Max HP |\n231: | | | **Moonstone** | +0.5% Crit Chance |\n232: | Rare | 12% | **Sapphire** | +1% Focus |\n233: | | | **Bloodstone** | 1% lifesteal (dungeon) |\n234: | Epic | 4.2% | **Star Ruby** | +3% Mining Power |\n235: | | | **Arcane Opal** | +3% XP gain |\n236: | Legendary | 0.8% | **Dragon's Eye** | +2 Pierce, +5% Crit Damage |\n237: | | | **Aether Diamond** | +1.5% to all stats |\n238: \n239: Gems drop as **Rough**. Combining **3 of the same gem** at the same cut into the **next cut** costs Arcane Dust + gold (Rough→Cut: 10 dust; Cut→Flawless: 30 dust). Gems are socketed into gear sockets (see Section 8) and can be swapped for a small gold fee.\n240: \n241: ### 6.2 The Gem Hollow (Once-a-Day Event)\n242: - **Availability:** once per local calendar day. The main-screen button shows READY or a countdown to local midnight.\n243: - **Scene:** a dark crystal cavern with **12 dull rock veins** in a grid; a lantern circle follows the cursor and lights nearby veins.\n244: - **Swings:** the player has **25 swings** (+1 per consecutive-day streak day, max +10). Each tap = 1 swing.\n245: - **Veins:** each vein has 1–3 durability. On break, a roll: **45% gem**, **35% Arcane Dust (2–6)**, **20% gold pouch**.\n246: - **Weak-point ring:** on some veins a ring shrinks; tapping while it is inside the target zone breaks the vein instantly and adds a **+1 gem** bonus.\n247: - **Luck** shifts the gem rarity weights upward by up to +25% of the weight of the tiers above Common.\n248: - **Expected result:** ~5–8 gems, occasionally a Legendary. Show a results summary with rarities highlighted.\n249: - **Rules:** progress is saved mid-event (if the user leaves, they can resume the same day); once finished or expired at midnight, it locks until the next day.\n250: \n251: ---\n252: \n253: ## 7. Player Progression\n254: \n255: ### 7.1 Level & XP\n256: - XP from breaking blocks, dungeon wins and boss clears.\n257: - `xpToNext(L) = round(40 × L^1.8)`. Level cap **60**, increased by Ascension.\n258: - Each level gives: **+1.5% Mining Power**, **+3 Max HP**, **+1 Attack**, and **1 Vow Point**.\n259: \n260: ### 7.2 Miner's Vows (Perk Tree)\n261: Spend Vow Points in three branches. Each node has ranks (max shown).\n262: \n263: | Branch | Node | Effect per rank | Max |\n264: |---|---|---|---|\n265: | **Delver** (mining) | Strong Arms | +3% Mining Power | 10 |\n266: | | Keen Eye | +1% Crit Chance | 10 |\n267: | | Prospector | +1 Luck | 10 |\n268: | | Deep Breath | Weak Spot appears 5% more often | 5 |\n269: | | Shatterer (capstone, Lv 30) | Every 25th hit is a guaranteed crit | 1 |\n270: | **Warrior** (combat) | Stout Heart | +4% Max HP | 10 |\n271: | | Whetstone | +3% Attack | 10 |\n272: | | Ironskin | +2% Defense | 10 |\n273: | | Swift Boots | +1 Speed | 5 |\n274: | | Second Wind (capstone, Lv 30) | Once per dungeon run, revive at 30% HP | 1 |\n275: | **Scholar** (economy) | Quick Study | +3% XP | 10 |\n276: | | Haggler | +3% gold from sales | 10 |\n277: | | Steady Hand | +1% Focus (campaign leniency) | 10 |\n278: | | Dust Collector | +2% Arcane Dust chance | 5 |\n279: | | Gem Whisperer (capstone, Lv 30) | +3 Gem Hollow swings | 1 |\n280: \n281: Provide a free **respec** button that costs gold (`level × 100`).\n282: \n283: ### 7.3 Stat Glossary\n284: | Stat | Category | Meaning |\n285: |---|---|---|\n286: | Mining Power | Mining | Base damage per click |\n287: | Crit Chance / Crit Damage | Mining & Combat | Chance and multiplier |\n288: | Pierce | Mining | Flat reduction of block hardness |\n289: | Luck | Mining | Rarity weight shift, double-drop chance, gem rarity |\n290: | Scholar (XP%) | Economy | Bonus XP |\n291: | Max HP | Combat & Campaign | Dungeon health |\n292: | Attack | Combat | Dungeon damage |\n293: | Defense | Combat | Damage reduction: `100/(100+Def)` |\n294: | Speed | Combat | Turn order |\n295: | **Focus** | Campaign | Widens timing windows and lowers thresholds by up to **+25%** |\n296: | **Ward** | Campaign | Extra hearts in boss fights, max **+2** |\n297: \n298: ---\n299: \n300: ## 8. Gear\n301: \n302: ### 8.1 Slots & Primary Stats\n303: Six slots. Each gear piece has a **tier (1–10)**, a **base stat set**, an **upgrade level** and **sockets**.\n304: \n305: | Slot | Primary Stats | Secondary Stats |\n306: |---|---|---|\n307: | **Pickaxe** | Mining Power, Attack | Pierce, Crit Chance, Crit Damage |\n308: | **Helm** | Max HP | Luck, Ward |\n309: | **Chestplate** | Defense, Max HP | Ward |\n310: | **Gauntlets** | Attack | Crit Chance, Focus |\n311: | **Boots** | Speed | Max HP, Focus |\n312: | **Amulet** | Luck | XP%, Crit Damage |\n313: \n314: ### 8.2 Stat Scaling by Tier\n315: `stat(tier) = baseStat × 2.3^(tier − 1)` (Pickaxe Power uses `3 × 2.6^(tier−1)` to match block HP).\n316: \n317: | Slot | Base (tier 1) |\n318: |---|---|\n319: | Pickaxe | Power 3, Attack 2, Pierce 0 (+1 per 2 tiers), Crit 5% |\n320: | Helm | Max HP 30, Luck 1 |\n321: | Chestplate | Defense 4, Max HP 50 |\n322: | Gauntlets | Attack 4, Crit 2% |\n323: | Boots | Speed 5 (+1.5 per tier, not exponential), Max HP 15 |\n324: | Amulet | Luck 2, XP% 3 |\n325: \n326: Ward comes only from Helm/Chestplate at tiers 4, 7, 10 (+1 each, hard-capped at +2 total). Focus comes from Gauntlets/Boots tiers 3, 6, 9 (+5% each, capped at 25% total incl. perks).\n327: \n328: ### 8.3 Gear Names by Tier (Fantasy Flavor)\n329: | Tier | Set Name | Pickaxe | Helm | Chestplate | Gauntlets | Boots | Amulet |\n330: |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n331: | 1 | Ashen | Stone Pick | Ash Cap | Ash Jerkin | Ash Wraps | Ash Sandals | Pebble Charm |\n332: | 2 | Copper | Copper Pick | Copper Coif | Copper Cuirass | Copper Grips | Copper Greaves | Copper Locket |\n333: | 3 | Iron | Iron Pick | Iron Helm | Iron Hauberk | Iron Gauntlets | Iron Sabatons | Iron Sigil |\n334: | 4 | Silver | Silver Pick | Silver Circlet | Silver Mail | Silver Gloves | Silver Treads | Silver Pendant |\n335: | 5 | Aurelite | Gilded Pick | Gilded Helm | Gilded Plate | Gilded Fists | Gilded Boots | Gilded Amulet |\n336: | 6 | Runic | Runepick | Rune Hood | Rune Robe | Rune Mitts | Rune Slippers | Rune Talisman |\n337: | 7 | Starmetal | Starbreaker | Star Helm | Star Plate | Star Gauntlets | Star Greaves | Star Choker |\n338: | 8 | Wyrm | Wyrmfang Pick | Wyrm Helm | Wyrm Hide | Wyrm Claws | Wyrm Boots | Wyrm Talon |\n339: | 9 | Void | Voidpick | Void Cowl | Void Shroud | Void Grasp | Void Steps | Void Eye |\n340: | 10 | Aether | Aetherpick | Aether Crown | Aether Plate | Aether Hands | Aether Stride | Aether Heart |\n341: \n342: ### 8.4 Upgrading\n343: - Upgrade levels **+0 → +10**. Each level adds **+8% of the piece's base stats** (so +10 = ×1.8).\n344: - Cost for level `k` (1–10) of a tier `t` piece: **gold** `20 × 3^(t−1) × k^1.5`, **ingots** of that tier `ceil(k/2) + 1`, **Arcane Dust** `k`.\n345: - Upgrades never fail (no frustration). Downgrade/refund is not allowed.\n346: - **Mythic levels (+11 to +20):** unlocked after beating the final boss. Each adds +10% base stats. Cost also needs **Aether Shards** (Deep Strata) and **Echo Sigils** (Echo bosses).\n347: \n348: ### 8.5 Sockets\n349: - Sockets by tier: 1–3: 0, 4–6: 1, 7–9: 2, 10: 3. Legendaries always have +1 extra.\n350: - Gems are socketed in the Inventory/Gear detail view; swapping costs `100 × tier` gold.\n351: \n352: ### 8.6 Legendary Set Bonus\n353: The number of **equipped legendary items** grants:\n354: - **2:** +10% Mining Power and +10% Luck\n355: - **4:** +25% Attack, Max HP and Defense; +1 Ward (counts toward the cap)\n356: - **6 (all slots):** \"Hollow Crowned\" — +50% XP, unique golden aura around the equipped-gear paperdoll and the mining pickaxe\n357: \n358: ---\n359: \n360: ## 9. Crafting\n361: \n362: ### 9.1 Screens & Flow\n363: Tabs: **Smelter**, **Forge**, **Gem Cutter**, **Enchant (later)**.\n364: \n365: **Smelter:** convert **4 ore → 1 ingot** of that tier, instant (with an animated furnace made of shapes). Bulk buttons: ×1, ×10, Max.\n366: \n367: **Forge:** list recipes for every unlocked tier; show cost, resulting stats, and a compare arrow versus the currently equipped piece. Crafting takes 1 s (a hammer strike animation) with a small delay for feel.\n368: \n369: ### 9.2 Recipes (each of the 10 tiers, 6 slots)\n370: | Slot | Ingots (tier) | Trophies (tier) | Extras | Gold |\n371: |---|---|---|---|---|\n372: | Pickaxe | 8 | 2 | — | `60 × 3^(t−1)` |\n373: | Helm | 6 | 3 | — | `40 × 3^(t−1)` |\n374: | Chestplate | 10 | 4 | — | `80 × 3^(t−1)` |\n375: | Gauntlets | 5 | 2 | — | `40 × 3^(t−1)` |\n376: | Boots | 5 | 2 | — | `40 × 3^(t−1)` |\n377: | Amulet | 3 | 2 | 1 gem (any) | `50 × 3^(t−1)` |\n378: \n379: Tier 1 exception: no trophies required (tutorial-friendly). Trophies come from the dungeon (Section 11), which links dungeon runs to crafting.\n380: \n381: ### 9.3 Enchanting (Phase 8+)\n382: Spend Arcane Dust + gold to add **one random bonus stat line** to a piece (e.g. +3% Crit, +2 Luck). Reroll costs increase. Locked behind Level 20.\n383: \n384: ---\n385: \n386: ## 10. Campaign (10 Bosses)\n387: \n388: Each boss is a **short skill mini-game** (30–90 s) on a canvas. Think Undertale attack patterns and Punch-Out reads.\n389: \n390: ### 10.1 Global Rules\n391: - **Unlock:** boss *n* requires boss *n−1* cleared and a minimum player level (`minLevel = layer's Min Lv`, recommended gear tier = *n*).\n392: - **Hearts:** you start every fight with **3 + Ward** hearts. Reaching 0 = defeat. Unlimited retries, no cost.\n393: - **Focus (assist):** all timing windows are multiplied by `(1 + focus)` and score thresholds divided by `(1 + focus)`. Focus is capped at 25% so skill stays relevant.\n394: - **Stars:** each boss has 1★ (clear), 2★ (good), 3★ (flawless). Each star gives a **one-time reward** (gold, gems, Boss Essence).\n395: - **Rewards:** first clear = big gold + XP + **guaranteed Legendary**. Repeat clears = smaller gold + trophies; 3★ repeats have a 5% chance of a Boss Essence.\n396: - **Presentation:** before each fight, a title card (shape-built boss silhouette), a one-line rule explanation, a \"Ready?\" button, then a 3-2-1 countdown. Every fight has a pause button and a \"Give Up\".\n397: - **Controls:** each mini-game supports **touch/mouse** and **keyboard** (listed below).\n398: \n399: ### 10.2 The Ten Bosses\n400: \n401: #### 1. Grubnok, the Burrow-King — *Ashen Warrens*\n402: - **Look:** big brown circle head, two triangle ears, jagged gold crown, tiny circle eyes.\n403: - **Challenge: Frenzy Dig (CPS test).** Tap the throne rock as fast as possible for **10 s**. A Rage bar fills as CPS rises.\n404: - **Clear:** average ≥ 5 CPS. 2★: ≥ 7. 3★: ≥ 9. (Thresholds divided by 1 + Focus.)\n405: - **Twist:** a golden nugget spawns randomly; tapping it gives +5 instant taps.\n406: - **Controls:** tap/click, or Space.\n407: \n408: #### 2. Sir Rattlebone — *Copper Crypt*\n409: - **Look:** stacked grey circles and rectangles forming a skeleton knight, visor as a dark rectangle, rounded shield.\n410: - **Challenge: Bone Duel (timing).** A needle sweeps across a bar; stop it inside the green zone. **8 rounds**; the zone shrinks from 30% to 8% width and needle speed rises.\n411: - **Rules:** a miss costs a heart. 3★: no misses and ≥ 4 center (\"perfect\") hits.\n412: - **Controls:** tap/click or Space.\n413: \n414: #### 3. Morwenna the Ashen Witch — *Iron Hollows*\n415: - **Look:** dark triangle robe, pointed hat, floating ember circles, a hooked staff.\n416: - **Challenge: Cinder Dance (4-lane rhythm, osu!mania style).** Notes fall down 4 lanes. Hit them on the line. **75 s, ~110 notes**, 110 BPM, defined in a chart file (`chart_morwenna.ts`: array of `{time, lane}`).\n417: - **Judgments:** Perfect ±45 ms, Good ±100 ms, otherwise Miss.\n418: - **Groove gauge:** starts at 50, +2 per hit, −6 per miss. Reaching 0 fails the fight (hearts are not used here).\n419: - **Clear:** accuracy ≥ 70% (`(perfect + 0.6×good)/total`). 2★ ≥ 85%. 3★ ≥ 95%.\n420: - **Controls:** keys `D F J K` or tap the 4 lanes. Beat cues come from a WebAudio metronome (synth clicks) so no audio files are needed.\n421: \n422: #### 4. Thessaly, the Bog Hydra — *Silvermire*\n423: - **Look:** three green necks (thick curved paths) with circle heads and yellow slit eyes, rising from a murky ellipse.\n424: - **Challenge: Head Hunt (whack-a-mole with decoys).** 3×3 holes, **35 s**. Real heads (green) pop up for 1.4 s down to 0.8 s. **Decoys** (blue, bubbly) pop up 30% of the time.\n425: - **Rules:** clear with ≥ 30 real hits. Tapping a decoy costs a heart. Missed real heads have no penalty. 3★: ≥ 45 hits and no heart lost.\n426: - **Controls:** tap/click; keyboard `1–9` on a numpad layout.\n427: \n428: #### 5. Aldric, the Gilded Knight — *Aurelite Keep*\n429: - **Look:** tall gold rectangle armor with a plume triangle, a huge circle shield and a long thin sword.\n430: - **Challenge: Parry Duel (reaction).** **24 attacks** telegraphed from **Left, Right or Up** (arrow flashes and the sword pose changes). Press the matching direction inside the window (600 ms shrinking to 350 ms).\n431: - **Feints:** from attack 12 on, some telegraphs are fakes (grey flash). Do **nothing** on those, or lose a heart.\n432: - **Rules:** wrong/late = −1 heart. Every 6th successful parry opens a **Counter window** (press Space/tap Attack) that damages Aldric (visual only).\n433: - **Clear:** survive all 24. 3★: no hearts lost.\n434: - **Controls:** arrow keys or swipe / tap zones on screen; Space/tap center for counter.\n435: \n436: #### 6. Isolde, Runewarden — *Runic Archives*\n437: - **Look:** hooded triangle robe, an open book (two quads), six glowing rune shapes orbiting her.\n438: - **Challenge: Rune Recall (Simon says).** Six runes, each a distinct shape and color (triangle, square, hexagon, circle, diamond, star). Watch a sequence, then repeat it. **10 rounds**, sequence length 3 → 12, show speed 600 ms → 350 ms per rune.\n439: - **Rules:** a wrong rune = −1 heart, and the round replays. 3★: no mistakes.\n440: - **Controls:** tap runes; keys `1–6`.\n441: \n442: #### 7. Seraphel, the Fallen Star — *Stargazer Spire*\n443: - **Look:** a large glowing 8-point star polygon with a soft radial-gradient halo and a cracked \"face\" made of two small dark shapes.\n444: - **Challenge: Star Trace (ordered pointing under pressure).** **40 s**. Constellations of 4–6 numbered stars appear; tap them **in order** before they fade (2 s each). Meteors (streaks) cross the screen; tapping a meteor costs a heart.\n445: - **Clear:** finish ≥ 8 constellations. 3★: ≥ 12 and no hearts lost.\n446: - **Controls:** tap/click only (mouse-friendly), optional keyboard number keys not required.\n447: \n448: #### 8. Ignathia, the Ember Wyrm — *Wyrmbone Caverns*\n449: - **Look:** a large ember-red serpentine body with bone-white spikes, a wedge head, and three layered scale plates; a glowing heart and lance share a vertical rail.\n450: - **Challenge:** **Heartpierce.** Hold Space or the mouse button to raise the lance and release to fall, tracking Ignathia's moving heart across three phases.\n451: - **Rules:** overlap fills the phase gauge; separation drains it. An empty gauge costs a heart and grants one second of invulnerability. Phase 3 adds telegraphed heart darts. Clear all three phases. 3★: no hearts lost and at least 70% overlap time.\n452: - **Controls:** hold Space or the left mouse button to rise; release to fall. Escape pauses.\n453: \n454: #### 9. Xar'Mal, the Lich Emperor — *Voidglass Sanctum*\n455: - **Look:** tall dark polygon robe, a skull made from circles and triangles, floating crown shards, violet fire.\n456: - **Challenge: Soul Dodge (bullet hell, Undertale-style).** **60 s**. The player controls a small heart (hitbox radius 5) inside a square arena. Patterns come in waves: **spirals, falling rain, sweeping lasers (0.8 s telegraph), orbiting rings**, defined in a scripted timeline (`pattern_xarmal.ts`).\n457: - **Rules:** on hit −1 heart, then 1 s of invincibility. Clear = survive 60 s. 3★: ≤ 1 hit taken.\n458: - **Controls:** mouse/touch drag (heart follows finger with offset), or arrow keys / WASD with `Shift` for slow-move.\n459: \n460: #### 10. The Hollow King — *The Aether Vault / Hollow Throne*\n461: - **Look:** giant empty armor (rectangles, no face), a floating crown ring of teal light, a hollow chest showing swirling stars.\n462: - **Challenge: The Five Trials (gauntlet).** Hearts are shared across all phases (3 + Ward; **1 heart is restored between phases 2 and 3**).\n463:   1. **Phase 1 — Burst:** 6 s CPS burst, need ≥ 8 CPS.\n464:   2. **Phase 2 — Rhythm:** 30 s, 4 lanes, groove gauge rules from Boss 3.\n465:   3. **Phase 3 — Parry:** 12 attacks with feints (Boss 5 rules).\n466:   4. **Phase 4 — Soul Dodge:** 40 s pattern remix (Boss 9 rules).\n467:   5. **Phase 5 — Crown Strike:** 3 timing hits with a 5% zone and fast needle.\n468: - **Clear:** finish all phases. 2★: ≤ 2 hearts lost. 3★: 0 hearts lost.\n469: \n470: ### 10.3 Legendary Drops (Guaranteed on First Clear)\n471: Base stats: **tier-*n* stats × 1.5**, +1 extra socket, upgradable like normal gear. Each has a unique effect.\n472: \n473: | # | Item | Slot | Unique Effect |\n474: |---|---|---|---|\n475: | 1 | **Burrowking's Crown** | Helm | **Tremor:** every 10th click deals ×3 damage. |\n476: | 2 | **Bonecleaver Gauntlets** | Gauntlets | **Rattling Rhythm:** consecutive crits stack +5% Crit Damage (max 10 stacks, reset on non-crit). |\n477: | 3 | **Cinderheart Amulet** | Amulet | **Cinderlight:** 8% per click to ignite the block for 3 s, dealing 40% of Power per second. |\n478: | 4 | **Hydrascale Cuirass** | Chestplate | **Regrowth:** heal 3% Max HP at the start of each dungeon round. |\n479: | 5 | **Sunforged Pickaxe** | Pickaxe | **Midas Strike:** 10% chance on block break to grant bonus gold equal to 5× that ore's sell value. |\n480: | 6 | **Runewarden's Diadem** | Helm | **Scholar's Insight:** +30% XP and Arcane Dust drops ×1.5. |\n481: | 7 | **Starfall Treads** | Boots | **Stargazer's Stride:** +15% Speed and always act first in round 1 of dungeon fights. |\n482: | 8 | **Wyrmfang Breaker** | Pickaxe | **Earthsplitter:** crits deal +100% damage to blocks above 50% HP. |\n483: | 9 | **Voidglass Phylactery** | Amulet | **Soul Siphon:** heal 5% of damage dealt in the dungeon; +20% Pierce effect on blocks. |\n484: | 10 | **Aetherbrand, the Hollow Pick** | Pickaxe | **Hollow Echo:** every click has a 15% chance to strike again for 60% damage (can chain once). Visually leaves a teal afterimage. |\n485: \n486: ### 10.4 Awakening\n487: Spend **Boss Essence** to awaken a legendary (I, II, III). Each level increases the unique effect's numbers by +25% and adds +10% base stats. Costs: 5 / 15 / 40 essence.\n488: \n489: ---\n490: \n491: ## 11. Dungeon (Auto-Battle for Gold)\n492: \n493: ### 11.1 Overview\n494: The player picks a **dungeon zone** (same 10 zones as the layers). Zone *n* unlocks when boss *n−1* is cleared (zone 1 always open). Press **Enter Dungeon** and a turn-based fight plays out **automatically** — no inputs beyond the speed toggle (×1, ×2, ×4) and Skip.\n495: \n496: ### 11.2 Run Structure\n497: - A run has **5 floors**. Floors 1–4 spawn 1–3 enemies; floor 5 is a **mini-boss**.\n498: - The full run is **simulated deterministically first** (seeded), then replayed visually. This makes Skip instant and tests trivial.\n499: - If the player dies, they keep half the gold and loot found so far; if they clear all 5 floors they keep all and get a **chest** (extra gold + chance for a gem or Arcane Dust).\n500: - Optional config flag `DUNGEON_TORCHES_ENABLED` (default `false`): a run costs 1 Torch, max 10, regen 1 per 5 min.\n501: \n502: ### 11.3 Combat Rules\n503: - **Round:** all combatants act in descending **Speed** order (ties: player first).\n504: - **Basic attack:** `damage = attack × rand(0.9, 1.1) × 100/(100 + targetDefense)`; crit uses the crit chance/damage stats.\n505: - **Enemy abilities** (simple, cooldown-based): *Heal* (restore 15% HP), *Poison* (5% of max HP over 3 rounds), *Guard* (halve next damage), *Frenzy* (+30% attack for 2 rounds).\n506: - **Player:** attacks automatically; legendary passives and gem effects trigger (Regrowth, Soul Siphon, Bloodstone lifesteal, etc.).\n507: - **Battle log:** compact text lines under the animation (e.g. \"You hit Cave Rat for 34!\").\n508: \n509: ### 11.4 Enemy Scaling (Zone n, Floor f 1–5)\n510: ```\n511: enemyHP      = 40  × 2.4^(n−1) × (1 + 0.15(f−1))\n512: enemyAttack  = 6   × 2.2^(n−1) × (1 + 0.10(f−1))\n513: enemyDefense = 4   × 2.1^(n−1)\n514: enemyGold    = 10  × 2.5^(n−1) × (1 + 0.10(f−1))\n515: enemySpeed   = 8 + 2n\n516: Mini-boss    = ×3 HP, ×1.5 Attack, ×3 Gold, and one extra ability\n517: ```\n518: Player stats come from gear and level; tune with a balance simulation script (`npm run balance`) that reports win rate and gold/min per zone for a \"typical\" gear set.\n519: \n520: ### 11.5 Zones, Enemies & Trophies\n521: | # | Zone | Regular Enemies (shapes) | Mini-Boss | Trophy Drop |\n522: |---|---|---|---|---|\n523: | 1 | Ashen Warrens | Cave Rat (small oval + tail), Goblin Grub, Dust Imp | Gnome Sergeant | Gnawed Pelt |\n524: | 2 | Copper Crypt | Skeleton Thrall, Crypt Bat, Green Slime | Bone Captain | Grave Bone |\n525: | 3 | Iron Hollows | Cinder Cultist, Fire Wisp, Iron Golem-lite | Ashen Priestess | Cinder Wick |\n526: | 4 | Silvermire | Bog Toad, Mire Wraith, Leech Swarm | Mire Matron | Bog Scale |\n527: | 5 | Aurelite Keep | Gilded Guard, Cursed Hound, Coin Mimic | Steward of Gold | Gilded Thread |\n528: | 6 | Runic Archives | Ink Wisp, Tome Mimic, Rune Sentinel | Archivist Shade | Ink Wisp |\n529: | 7 | Stargazer Spire | Comet Sprite, Moonlit Gargoyle, Star Weaver | Astral Watcher | Star Shard |\n530: | 8 | Wyrmbone Caverns | Drake Whelp, Ember Salamander, Bone Wyrmling | Wyrm Warden | Wyrm Scale |\n531: | 9 | Voidglass Sanctum | Shadow Acolyte, Void Leech, Hollow Knight | Void Reaver | Void Ichor |\n532: | 10 | The Aether Vault | Aether Phantom, Crown Shard, Vault Warden | Echo of the King | Aether Mote |\n533: \n534: Each enemy is drawn from 3–6 primitives, using zone color palettes.\n535: \n536: ### 11.6 Rewards\n537: - **Gold** per enemy (formula above), scaled by `1 + Haggler%`.\n538: - **Trophies:** 1–2 per floor cleared.\n539: - **XP:** `enemyGold × 0.5`.\n540: - **Chest (run clear):** gold ×3, 15% Arcane Dust, 5% gem (Rough).\n541: \n542: ---\n543: \n544: ## 12. Post-Campaign Scaling (After All 10 Bosses)\n545: \n546: ### 12.1 Echo Campaign\n547: After beating the Hollow King, the campaign unlocks **Echo Ranks**. Bosses return in a fight against their \"Echo\" (purple-tinted variants).\n548: - **Rank R:** all bosses get modifiers `mod = 1 + 0.08 × (R − 1)`, capped at 2.0: faster needles/patterns, narrower windows, higher CPS thresholds.\n549: - **Rewards:** `1 + 0.25 × (R − 1)` × base gold; each Echo boss clear grants **Echo Sigils** (used for Mythic upgrades and Ascension boosts).\n550: - **Rank up:** clear all 10 Echo bosses at rank R to unlock R + 1. Echo Focus is capped lower (15%) so it does not trivialize things.\n551: - Hearts stay the same (3 + Ward), so mastery matters.\n552: \n553: ### 12.2 Deep Strata & Abyss\n554: - **Deep Strata:** endless mining depth in the Aether Vault (see Section 5).\n555: - **The Abyss:** endless dungeon; each floor is +8% stronger than the last, a mini-boss every 10 floors, weekly-seeded. Rewards Aether Motes, Echo Sigils and a leaderboard-style personal best (local only).\n556: \n557: ### 12.3 Mythic Gear\n558: Levels +11 to +20 (Section 8.4) and a fourth socket on Tier 10 gear. Mythic gear gets a subtle animated glow.\n559: \n560: ### 12.4 Ascension (Prestige)\n561: - **Unlocks** after clearing the Hollow King once (recommended at Echo Rank 2).\n562: - **Resets:** player level, gold, materials, non-legendary gear, Vow Points.\n563: - **Keeps:** legendaries (and their Awakening), gems, campaign/Echo progress, achievements, settings.\n564: - **Reward:** `Ember Marks = floor(sqrt(goldEarnedThisRun / 50,000)) + 2 × EchoRank`.\n565: - **Ember Perks (permanent):** +5% Mining Power / gold / XP per rank (max 20), +5 level cap per 3 ranks (up to 100), start each run with a tier-3 gear set, and faster smelting.\n566: \n567: ---\n568: \n569: ## 13. Other Screens\n570: \n571: ### 13.1 Inventory\n572: - Tabs: **Ore**, **Ingots**, **Gems**, **Gear**, **Misc/Key items**.\n573: - Item cards show icon (shapes), name, count, sell value. Tap for details; **Sell**, **Sell All Duplicates**, **Equip**, **Socket**.\n574: - Gear detail: stats, upgrade level, sockets, comparison against equipped. Lock icon prevents accidental selling.\n575: - Sorting/filtering by tier and rarity.\n576: \n577: ### 13.2 Profile\n578: - Character paperdoll: a simple figure made from shapes with the six equipped items drawn on it.\n579: - Level, XP bar, all computed stats (with source breakdown on tap).\n580: - **Vows** tree tab.\n581: - **Lifetime stats:** blocks broken, clicks, gold earned, gems found, bosses cleared (with stars), dungeon runs, playtime.\n582: - **Achievements** (~30): e.g. \"Break 1,000 blocks\", \"First Legendary\", \"3★ all bosses\", \"Reach Echo Rank 5\".\n583: - **Settings:** volume, hold-to-mine, screen shake, number format, export/import save, hard reset.\n584: \n585: ### 13.3 Campaign Screen\n586: - A vertical path map (SVG nodes connected by lines) showing 10 boss nodes: locked (grey), available (pulsing), cleared (gold with stars). Tap a node for a boss card: lore line, challenge description, your best stars, recommended level/gear tier, rewards and **Fight**.\n587: \n588: ---\n589: \n590: ## 14. Backend & Online Services\n591: \n592: ### 14.1 Principles\n593: - **Offline-first.** Solo play is fully local. Online features need an account and a connection.\n594: - **Server-authoritative where competition is involved:** PvP Arena, Abyss leaderboard, Guild Colossus, daily event claims, seasons, guild state.\n595: - **Client-authoritative for solo progress,** protected by plausibility checks (Section 19). Cheating alone is possible but cannot reach competitive boards.\n596: - **Self-hosted first:** one Docker Compose stack on a single machine. Design so it *could* scale later (stateless API, Redis optional).\n597: \n598: ### 14.2 Architecture\n599: ```\n600:  Client (C++/raylib)\n601:    ├─ HTTPS REST ─────────────▶ Caddy (TLS) ─▶ API (Drogon, C++) ─▶ PostgreSQL 16\n602:    └─ WSS (chat, notifications) ▶                    │\n603:                                                      └─▶ Redis (optional: rate limits, cache)\n604:  Jobs inside the API process (scheduler): season rollover, weekly resets, leaderboard cache refresh,\n605:  save retention, inactive-leader transfer. Nightly backup container: pg_dump.\n606: ```\n607: \n608: ### 14.3 API Conventions\n609: - Base path `/v1`, JSON bodies, `Authorization: Bearer <access token>`.\n610: - Errors: `{ \"error\": { \"code\": \"string_code\", \"message\": \"human text\" } }` with proper HTTP status.\n611: - Every request sends `X-Client-Version`; the server can answer `426 Upgrade Required`.\n612: - Requests that grant anything (event claims, arena attacks, inbox claims) require an **`Idempotency-Key`** header and are safe to retry.\n613: - Lists use cursor pagination (`?limit=50&cursor=…`). Max body: 512 KB.\n614: - `GET /healthz` (liveness, DB ping) and optional `GET /metrics` (Prometheus text).\n615: \n616: ### 14.4 Endpoint Overview\n617: | Area | Endpoints |\n618: |---|---|\n619: | Auth | `POST /auth/guest`, `POST /auth/register` (link/upgrade), `POST /auth/login`, `POST /auth/refresh`, `POST /auth/logout`, `POST /auth/password`, `DELETE /account` |\n620: | Time | `GET /time` (server UTC, used for clock offset) |\n621: | Saves | `GET /saves/latest`, `PUT /saves` (conditional), `GET /saves/history`, `POST /saves/{revision}/restore` |\n622: | Profile | `GET /profile/me`, `GET /profile/{publicId}`, `PUT /profile/settings` (timezone offset, display name) |\n623: | Daily event | `POST /events/gem-hollow/start`, `POST /events/gem-hollow/claim` |\n624: | Guilds | see Section 16.7 |\n625: | Arena | see Section 17.9 |\n626: | Leaderboards | see Section 18.4 |\n627: | Inbox | `GET /inbox`, `POST /inbox/{id}/claim` (season rewards, Colossus rewards, moderator messages) |\n628: | Reports | `POST /reports` (player, chat message, guild) |\n629: | WebSocket | `GET /ws` (auth by first message) — channels: `guild:{id}`, `user:{id}` |\n630: \n631: ### 14.5 Database Schema (PostgreSQL, condensed)\n632: Migrations are numbered SQL files (`0001_init.sql`, …) applied by a small runner in the server at startup, tracked in `schema_migrations`. All queries are parameterized.\n633: \n634: ```sql\n635: CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS citext;  CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS pgcrypto;\n636: \n637: CREATE TABLE accounts (\n638:   id BIGSERIAL Please note that any changes targeting the original code should remove the line number, colon, and leading space.\n1: # Emberdeep Project Guide\n2: \n3: This document is the technical and gameplay reference for the current Emberdeep\n4: repository. It describes the architecture, source layout, runtime data flow,\n5: game systems, boss campaign, persistence, backend, controls, tests, and the\n6: work completed during the development session that produced this version.\n7: \n8: The repository is a C++20/raylib mining, progression, crafting, equipment,\n9: dungeon, and ten-boss campaign game. The deterministic gameplay rules live in a\n10: shared `core` library. The graphical client, terminal playtest, and server\n11: reuse that library rather than maintaining separate gameplay implementations.\n12: \n13: ## 1. Project identity and design principles\n14: \n15: Emberdeep is an offline-first procedural mining/clicker game set in a\n16: multi-layer underground world. The intended loop is:\n17: \n18: 1. Mine the current block.\n19: 2. Collect ore, gems, gold, trophies, experience, and progression currencies.\n20: 3. Smelt ore and craft equipment.\n21: 4. Upgrade and equip gear.\n22: 5. Enter dungeons and campaign boss challenges.\n23: 6. Unlock deeper layers and stronger materials.\n24: 7. Complete the ten-boss campaign and continue through post-campaign systems.\n25: \n26: Important design principles:\n27: \n28: - **Shared rules:** gameplay calculations are in `core`, not duplicated in the\n29:   clients.\n30: - **Determinism:** seeded PCG32 randomness is used for minigames and dungeon\n31:   simulation so tests and playtest scenarios can be reproduced.\n32: - **Procedural visuals:** the graphical client uses raylib primitives and text;\n33:   boss visuals do not depend on a sprite-heavy boss asset pipeline.\n34: - **Offline-first saves:** local saves remain usable without the backend.\n35: - **Progression gates:** layers and bosses require appropriate level,\n36:   pickaxe, and previous-campaign progress.\n37: - **Atomic persistence:** save writes use temporary files and replacement\n38:   rather than exposing partially-written save files.\n39: - **Data-driven definitions:** materials, layers, gems, bosses, perks, and\n40:   dungeon zones also have JSON/data representations, while the current C++\n41:   core has compiled default definitions used by the clients and tests.\n42: \n43: The repository intentionally ignores TypeScript-related implementation hints\n44: from earlier specification drafts. The implemented project is C++20.\n45: \n46: ## 2. Top-level repository layout\n47: \n48: ```text\n49: .\n50: ├── CMakeLists.txt\n51: ├── CMakePresets.json\n52: ├── vcpkg.json\n53: ├── README.md\n54: ├── EMBERDEEP_PROJECT_GUIDE.md       # This document\n55: ├── EMBERDEEP GAME SPEC NEW.md       # Updated game specification\n56: ├── EMBERDEEP GAME SPEC NEW copy.md  # Copy of the updated specification\n57: ├── BOSS_8_IGNATHIA_SPEC.md          # Heartpierce source specification\n58: ├── bosses.md                        # Boss implementation plan/reference\n59: ├── core/\n60: │   ├── include/ed/                  # Public shared-core headers\n61: │   └── src/                        # Shared gameplay and persistence code\n62: ├── client/\n63: │   ├── src/app/main.cpp             # Main raylib application\n64: │   ├── src/playtest/main.cpp        # Terminal playtest executable\n65: │   └── src/screens/                 # Lightweight client screen support\n66: ├── server/\n67: │   ├── src/                         # API, save backend, PostgreSQL backend\n68: │   ├── migrations/                  # PostgreSQL schema migrations\n69: │   └── postgres-init/               # Database role/init/upgrade scripts\n70: ├── tests/\n71: │   └── core/                        # Catch2 unit and regression tests\n72: ├── data/                            # JSON definitions and content data\n73: ├── deploy/                          # Caddy, Docker, and operations docs\n74: ├── sprites/                         # Mining/material sprite assets\n75: └── docs/\n76:     └── DECISIONS.md                 # Recorded architecture decisions\n77: ```\n78: \n79: The checked-in `build/` directory is generated output and FetchContent state.\n80: It is not source architecture and should not be edited manually. Local files\n81: such as `.env`, `emberdeep.profile`, and `.sav` files contain environment or\n82: player state and must not be committed.\n83: \n84: ## 3. Build graph and target relationships\n85: \n86: The root `CMakeLists.txt` enables C++20, exports compile commands, adds `core`,\n87: optionally adds `client`, always adds `server`, and adds `tests`.\n88: \n89: ### 3.1 `emberdeep_core`\n90: \n91: Defined in `core/CMakeLists.txt` and built from:\n92: \n93: ```text\n94: campaign.cpp\n95: boss_minigames.cpp\n96: crafting.cpp\n97: dungeon.cpp\n98: formulas.cpp\n99: game_state.cpp\n100: inventory.cpp\n101: postgame.cpp\n102: pcg.cpp\n103: save.cpp\n104: stats.cpp\n105: cloud.cpp\n106: ```\n107: \n108: This library contains the authoritative rules for:\n109: \n110: - player/game state;\n111: - mining and block generation;\n112: - material and progression formulas;\n113: - inventory, equipment, sockets, and gems;\n114: - crafting and upgrades;\n115: - campaign progression and boss outcomes;\n116: - all boss minigame state machines;\n117: - dungeons and post-game systems;\n118: - local save serialization;\n119: - cloud-save client communication.\n120: \n121: ### 3.2 `emberdeep_client`\n122: \n123: Defined in `client/CMakeLists.txt`. It links to `emberdeep_core` and includes\n124: screen support in `client/src/screens/main_screen.cpp`.\n125: \n126: The same CMake target produces:\n127: \n128: - `emberdeep_playtest`, the terminal-oriented playtest;\n129: - `emberdeep_graphical`, the raylib graphical client.\n130: \n131: The graphical target additionally requires raylib.\n132: \n133: ### 3.3 `emberdeep_server` and `emberdeep_api`\n134: \n135: `emberdeep_server` links to `emberdeep_core` and contains the save backend.\n136: `emberdeep_api` contains the HTTP API and links OpenSSL for hashing. PostgreSQL\n137: support is optional and enabled with:\n138: \n139: ```text\n140: -DEMBERDEEP_ENABLE_POSTGRES=ON\n141: ```\n142: \n143: When enabled, the API also compiles `postgres_backend.cpp` and links the\n144: PostgreSQL client library.\n145: \n146: ### 3.4 Tests\n147: \n148: `tests/CMakeLists.txt` fetches Catch2 3.7.1 and builds\n149: `emberdeep_core_tests`. Tests link only against the shared core and Catch2.\n150: CTest discovers all Catch2 cases automatically.\n151: \n152: ## 4. Shared-core file reference\n153: \n154: ### `core/include/ed/types.hpp`\n155: \n156: Defines reusable data-only definitions:\n157: \n158: - `MaterialDef`;\n159: - `LayerDef`;\n160: - `GemDef`;\n161: - `VowNodeDef`;\n162: - `PlayerProgress`;\n163: - `MiningResult`;\n164: - list aliases such as `MaterialList`, `LayerList`, `GemList`, and\n165:   `VowNodeList`.\n166: \n167: ### `core/include/ed/data_model.hpp`\n168: \n169: Provides compiled default content:\n170: \n171: - ten materials from Ashen Stone through Aetherium;\n172: - ten layers from Ashen Warrens through The Aether Vault;\n173: - ten gems;\n174: - vow nodes for Delver, Warrior, and Scholar branches.\n175: \n176: The default gem list is:\n177: \n178: | ID | Name | Rarity | Effect |\n179: |---|---|---|---|\n180: | `amber` | Amber | common | +1 Luck |\n181: | `garnet` | Garnet | common | +2% Attack |\n182: | `jade` | Jade | uncommon | +2% Max HP |\n183: | `moonstone` | Moonstone | uncommon | +0.5% Crit Chance |\n184: | `sapphire` | Sapphire | rare | +1% Focus |\n185: | `bloodstone` | Bloodstone | rare | 1% lifesteal |\n186: | `star-ruby` | Star Ruby | epic | +3% Mining Power |\n187: | `arcane-opal` | Arcane Opal | epic | +3% XP gain |\n188: | `dragon-eye` | Dragon's Eye | legendary | +2 Pierce, +5% Crit Damage |\n189: | `aether-diamond` | Aether Diamond | legendary | +1.5% to all stats |\n190: \n191: `data/gems.json` contains the corresponding data-driven form. The JSON and\n192: compiled defaults should be kept synchronized when content changes.\n193: \n194: ### `core/include/ed/game_state.hpp` and `core/src/game_state.cpp`\n195: \n196: `PlayerState` stores level, XP, gold, Arcane Dust, boss/post-game currencies,\n197: vow points, mining counters, boss clears, ascensions, echo rank, deepest depth,\n198: pickaxe tier, and current layer.\n199: \n200: `GameState` contains:\n201: \n202: - `schemaVersion`;\n203: - `PlayerState player`;\n204: - a dirty flag;\n205: - a save identifier.\n206: \n207: The file also owns layer unlock checks, XP application, and game-state\n208: serialization/deserialization.\n209: \n210: ### `core/include/ed/inventory.hpp` and `core/src/inventory.cpp`\n211: \n212: The inventory model contains maps for:\n213: \n214: - ore;\n215: - ingots;\n216: - gems;\n217: - trophies.\n218: \n219: It also contains a vector of `GearPiece` objects and a `GearSet` of equipped\n220: item IDs.\n221: \n222: `GearPiece` contains:\n223: \n224: - slot and item ID;\n225: - display name;\n226: - tier;\n227: - upgrade level;\n228: - total socket count;\n229: - legendary flag;\n230: - ordered `socketedGems` IDs.\n231: \n232: Important operations:\n233: \n234: - `addItemToInventory`;\n235: - `removeItemFromInventory`;\n236: - `countItem`;\n237: - `equipGear`;\n238: - `gearSocketCount`;\n239: - `insertGem`;\n240: - `removeGem`.\n241: \n242: Socket rules currently grant:\n243: \n244: - tier 1-3: zero sockets;\n245: - tier 4-6: one socket;\n246: - tier 7-9: two sockets;\n247: - tier 10: three sockets;\n248: - legendary gear receives one additional socket.\n249: \n250: Insertion validates that the gem exists in inventory and that the piece has an\n251: empty socket before consuming one gem. Removal validates the socket index,\n252: returns the gem to inventory, and removes it from the equipment piece.\n253: \n254: The graphical Inventory screen currently uses:\n255: \n256: - `G` to insert the first available gem into the selected piece;\n257: - `R` to remove the selected piece's last socketed gem;\n258: - `E` to equip;\n259: - `U` to upgrade;\n260: - `A`/`D` to select gear;\n261: - `W`/`S` or mouse wheel to scroll.\n262: \n263: The current UI deliberately chooses the first gem in the map rather than\n264: opening a gem-selection dialog. This is functional but is a future UX\n265: improvement opportunity.\n266: \n267: ### `core/include/ed/stats.hpp` and `core/src/stats.cpp`\n268: \n269: `PlayerStats` is the derived-stat output used by mining, bosses, dungeons, and\n270: the Profile screen. It includes:\n271: \n272: - mining power;\n273: - mining-power percentage bonus;\n274: - attack;\n275: - defense;\n276: - max HP;\n277: - speed;\n278: - luck;\n279: - pierce;\n280: - crit chance basis points;\n281: - crit damage basis points;\n282: - XP bonus basis points;\n283: - focus basis points;\n284: - lifesteal basis points.\n285: \n286: `calculatePlayerStats` starts with level-based base stats, applies each\n287: equipped gear piece, applies upgrade and tier multipliers, then applies all\n288: socketed gem effects from equipped gear.\n289: \n290: Percentage gem effects are accumulated first and applied after all equipment\n291: totals are assembled. This avoids order-dependent rounding:\n292: \n293: - Garnet contributes attack percentage basis points;\n294: - Jade contributes max-HP percentage basis points;\n295: - Aether Diamond contributes percentage bonuses across the supported stats.\n296: \n297: Basis points use 100 basis points per percentage point. For example, 500\n298: crit-chance basis points means 5%.\n299: \n300: ### `core/include/ed/formulas.hpp` and `core/src/formulas.cpp`\n301: \n302: Contains shared formulas for:\n303: \n304: - material generation and layer scaling;\n305: - mining damage;\n306: - XP progression;\n307: - progress score;\n308: - reward calculations and related numeric rules.\n309: \n310: The mining/client code calls these formulas rather than implementing local\n311: copies.\n312: \n313: ### `core/include/ed/mining.hpp` and `core/src/mining.cpp`\n314: \n315: Owns block generation and mining interactions. Layer/material definitions\n316: determine hardness, HP, XP, sell value, and material identity. Mining applies\n317: player stats, hardness, critical behavior, weak spots, block damage, resource\n318: rewards, and progression counters.\n319: \n320: The graphical client uses this layer for the main mining screen. Successful\n321: layer changes spawn a new block; failed or boundary/locked navigation does not\n322: reset the current block.\n323: \n324: ### `core/include/ed/crafting.hpp` and `core/src/crafting.cpp`\n325: \n326: Defines craft recipes and operations for:\n327: \n328: - recipe generation;\n329: - smelting four matching ore into one ingot;\n330: - crafting gear;\n331: - upgrade cost calculation;\n332: - gear upgrades.\n333: \n334: Crafting consumes tier-specific ingots, trophies, gold, and—in recipes that\n335: require it—a gem. Crafted equipment receives its tier-derived socket count.\n336: Amulets are the current recipe family that requires a gem during crafting;\n337: that consumed gem is separate from gems later inserted into sockets.\n338: \n339: Upgrades consume gold and Arcane Dust and increase `upgradeLevel`, which feeds\n340: into equipment stat multipliers.\n341: \n342: ### `core/include/ed/dungeon.hpp` and `core/src/dungeon.cpp`\n343: \n344: Defines dungeon player stats, zones, floors, and deterministic dungeon run\n345: simulation. A run returns gold/XP/rewards and can find a gem. Dungeon content\n346: is represented in `data/dungeon_zones.json` and compiled/default structures.\n347: \n348: The terminal playtest exercises deterministic dungeon behavior, while the\n349: graphical client presents a simple zone-selection/run screen.\n350: \n351: ### `core/include/ed/campaign.hpp` and `core/src/campaign.cpp`\n352: \n353: Defines:\n354: \n355: - `BossChallenge`;\n356: - `BossDef`;\n357: - `BossAttempt`;\n358: - `BossOutcome`;\n359: - `CampaignState`.\n360: \n361: The current challenge enum is:\n362: \n363: ```text\n364: FrenzyDig\n365: BoneDuel\n366: CinderDance\n367: HeadHunt\n368: ParryDuel\n369: RuneRecall\n370: StarTrace\n371: Heartpierce\n372: SoulDodge\n373: FiveTrials\n374: ```\n375: \n376: Campaign logic handles:\n377: \n378: - ten-boss definitions;\n379: - sequential unlocks;\n380: - level and previous-clear requirements;\n381: - score thresholds;\n382: - star ratings;\n383: - first-clear rewards;\n384: - repeat-clear rewards;\n385: - campaign persistence.\n386: \n387: The code uses `Heartpierce` for Ignathia. The legacy `data/bosses.json` entry\n388: still says `\"wyrmfire-run\"` for Ignathia, so that JSON entry is stale relative\n389: to the current C++ implementation and should be updated if JSON becomes an\n390: active runtime source.\n391: \n392: Current campaign order:\n393: \n394: | # | Boss | Layer | Challenge | Level |\n395: |---:|---|---|---|---:|\n396: | 1 | Grubnok, the Burrow-King | Ashen Warrens | Frenzy Dig | 1 |\n397: | 2 | Sir Rattlebone | Copper Crypt | Bone Duel | 5 |\n398: | 3 | Morwenna the Ashen Witch | Iron Hollows | Cinder Dance | 10 |\n399: | 4 | Thessaly, the Bog Hydra | Silvermire | Head Hunt | 15 |\n400: | 5 | Aldric, the Gilded Knight | Aurelite Keep | Parry Duel | 20 |\n401: | 6 | Isolde, Runewarden | Runic Archives | Rune Recall | 27 |\n402: | 7 | Seraphel, the Fallen Star | Stargazer Spire | Star Trace | 34 |\n403: | 8 | Ignathia, the Ember Wyrm | Wyrmbone Caverns | Heartpierce | 42 |\n404: | 9 | Xar'Mal, the Lich Emperor | Voidglass Sanctum | Soul Dodge | 50 |\n405: | 10 | The Hollow King | The Aether Vault | Five Trials | 60 |\n406: \n407: ### `core/include/ed/boss_minigames.hpp` and `core/src/boss_minigames.cpp`\n408: \n409: This is the largest gameplay state-machine file. Every boss minigame has\n410: deterministic state, start/update/input/score functions as appropriate, and no\n411: raylib dependency. The client renders and routes input to these states.\n412: \n413: Implemented minigames:\n414: \n415: #### Frenzy Dig\n416: \n417: The player rapidly taps visible nugget targets. Spawn limits and target\n418: placement were tuned to avoid impossible/overlapping arrangements. Attack and\n419: gear-derived values affect scoring.\n420: \n421: #### Bone Duel\n422: \n423: A timing/strike duel with a moving needle and focus-scaled success zone.\n424: Starting side randomization and miss/heart behavior were corrected during\n425: development.\n426: \n427: #### Cinder Dance\n428: \n429: A rhythm chart with notes, lane judgment, groove, timing windows, and feedback.\n430: It supports standalone mode and a configurable gauntlet mode. Gauntlet mode\n431: prevents immediate phase failure when groove reaches zero so the Five Trials\n432: controller can own the phase result.\n433: \n434: The state supports:\n435: \n436: - configurable duration;\n437: - configurable note count;\n438: - four lanes;\n439: - `PERFECT`, `GOOD`, and `MISS` feedback;\n440: - feedback lane and timer;\n441: - deterministic chart generation.\n442: \n443: #### Head Hunt\n444: \n445: A grid of real and decoy targets. Selecting decoys or missing targets affects\n446: hearts and score.\n447: \n448: #### Parry Duel\n449: \n450: A direction-response duel with left/down/right attacks, reaction windows,\n451: heart loss, attack count, and feints.\n452: \n453: The Hollow King uses a 12-attack version. A feint is intentionally answered by\n454: waiting; timing out a feint advances the attack without costing a heart.\n455: Incorrect directional input costs a heart. The graphical client supports arrow\n456: keys and clickable direction panels.\n457: \n458: #### Rune Recall\n459: \n460: A deterministic sequence-memory challenge. Sequences are randomized per round,\n461: grow with progression, and are capped to a readable maximum.\n462: \n463: #### Star Trace\n464: \n465: The player clicks highlighted stars in order. Each round generates a fresh\n466: randomized placement rather than appending onto stale positions. Overlapping\n467: star placement was corrected, and later sequences are long enough to remain\n468: meaningful.\n469: \n470: #### Heartpierce\n471: \n472: Heartpierce replaced the old Wyrmfire Run after the revised\n473: `BOSS_8_IGNATHIA_SPEC.md` was adopted.\n474: \n475: Core mechanics:\n476: \n477: - hold to rise and release to fall;\n478: - a lance/player vertical position;\n479: - a moving heart waypoint;\n480: - overlap gauge;\n481: - multiple phases with different target movement parameters;\n482: - slower and less frequent retargeting than the initial implementation;\n483: - heart loss and temporary invulnerability;\n484: - phase 3 heart darts with telegraphing;\n485: - Focus-based overlap widening;\n486: - Echo/gear scaling through the surrounding campaign/stat systems.\n487: \n488: The visible UI includes the rail, target heart, lance, overlap zone, gauge,\n489: phase, timer, hearts, and controls.\n490: \n491: #### Soul Dodge\n492: \n493: Xar'Mal's challenge is a deterministic bullet-hell survival encounter.\n494: \n495: State includes:\n496: \n497: - normalized player coordinates;\n498: - duration and elapsed time;\n499: - hearts, hits, and invulnerability timer;\n500: - deterministic pattern state and wave timer;\n501: - active hazards;\n502: - slow/precision movement state;\n503: - finished/cleared flags.\n504: \n505: Hazard types:\n506: \n507: - spiral projectiles;\n508: - rain projectiles;\n509: - lasers with telegraphs;\n510: - expanding ring hazards.\n511: \n512: Non-laser waves spawn multiple objects. The number of objects was preserved\n513: while spatial spread was widened so waves are less clustered. Collision\n514: checking uses the same normalized positions rendered by the client:\n515: \n516: - laser collision checks horizontal distance to its lane;\n517: - rain checks point distance;\n518: - spiral/ring checks the calculated angle/radius position;\n519: - a successful hit removes one heart and starts one second of invulnerability.\n520: \n521: The standalone encounter lasts 60 seconds. The Hollow King remix uses a shorter\n522: 40-second duration.\n523: \n524: #### Crown Strike\n525: \n526: The final Hollow King phase is a timing challenge with a moving needle and\n527: three strikes. The success zone is centered around 0.5 and is currently 10%\n528: wide before Focus scaling, matching the wider visual green zone. Three\n529: successful strikes clear the phase.\n530: \n531: #### Five Trials\n532: \n533: The Hollow King controller shares hearts across five phases:\n534: \n535: 1. **Burst:** six-second rapid-tap trial requiring 48 taps.\n536: 2. **Rhythm:** 30-second Cinder Dance chart with 55 notes, four constrained\n537:    lanes, receptors, D/F/J/K input, and feedback.\n538: 3. **Parry:** 12-attack Parry Duel with visible direction/feint instructions.\n539: 4. **Soul Dodge remix:** 40-second Soul Dodge arena with shared-heart display\n540:    and visible hazards.\n541: 5. **Crown Strike:** three timing strikes.\n542: \n543: One heart is restored after phase 2, capped at the initial maximum. Failed\n544: phases or depletion of shared hearts end the attempt.\n545: \n546: The temporary developer phase-skip key was removed after boss development was\n547: complete. There are no remaining `]`, F6, or campaign phase-skip references in\n548: the current client.\n549: \n550: ### `core/include/ed/pcg.hpp` and `core/src/pcg.cpp`\n551: \n552: Implements the deterministic PCG32 random generator used by minigames,\n553: dungeons, target placement, and other seeded systems. Seeds are kept in state\n554: or passed to start functions so test cases can compare independent runs.\n555: \n556: ### `core/include/ed/postgame.hpp` and `core/src/postgame.cpp`\n557: \n558: Contains ascension, Echo, post-campaign, and other long-term progression\n559: operations. These systems consume campaign/progression state and update\n560: run-scoped and account-scoped currencies.\n561: \n562: ### `core/include/ed/save.hpp` and `core/src/save.cpp`\n563: \n564: `SaveSnapshot` groups:\n565: \n566: - `GameState`;\n567: - `InventoryState`;\n568: - `CampaignState`.\n569: \n570: Serialization is a line-oriented text format. It stores game state, resource\n571: maps, campaign arrays, gear fields, socketed gem IDs, and equipped IDs.\n572: Socketed gems were added as a backward-compatible optional eighth gear field:\n573: \n574: ```text\n575: gear=slot|itemId|name|tier|upgradeLevel|sockets|legendary|gem1,gem2\n576: ```\n577: \n578: Older seven-field gear lines remain readable. Local save writes are atomic.\n579: The serializer is also used by cloud-save requests.\n580: \n581: ### `core/include/ed/cloud.hpp` and `core/src/cloud.cpp`\n582: \n583: Implements the client-side cloud-save request path. The graphical client can\n584: upload/download serialized snapshots through the authenticated API while still\n585: maintaining local account-bound saves.\n586: \n587: ## 5. Graphical client architecture\n588: \n589: `client/src/app/main.cpp` is the main application and currently owns:\n590: \n591: - raylib window and main loop;\n592: - `AppState`;\n593: - screen selection;\n594: - input routing;\n595: - all active minigame states;\n596: - campaign attempt initialization;\n597: - rendering for mining, inventory, crafting, campaign, dungeon, profile, and\n598:   account-related flows;\n599: - autosave/manual save behavior;\n600: - local/cloud account flows.\n601: \n602: The client runs update logic in `updateEffects`, routes active minigame input\n603: through `handleActiveCampaignInput`, and renders through screen-specific\n604: functions such as `drawCampaignScreen`, `drawInventoryScreen`,\n605: `drawProfileScreen`, and `drawCraftingScreen`.\n606: \n607: ### Active fight input isolation\n608: \n609: When a boss attempt is active, boss input is processed before global controls.\n610: This prevents movement/navigation, sign-out, save controls, and unrelated\n611: screen actions from accidentally affecting the fight. `BACKSPACE` abandons an\n612: active attempt.\n613: \n614: ### Profile stats screen\n615: \n616: The Profile menu now calls `calculatePlayerStats` and displays:\n617: \n618: - mining power and mining bonus;\n619: - attack, defense, max HP, speed, pierce, luck;\n620: - crit chance and crit damage;\n621: - focus, XP bonus, and lifesteal.\n622: \n623: The displayed values include equipped gear and socketed gem effects.\n624: \n625: ### Inventory gem workflow\n626: \n627: 1. Open Inventory.\n628: 2. Select a gear piece with `A`/`D` or the mouse.\n629: 3. Press `G` to insert the first available inventory gem.\n630: 4. Press `R` to remove the selected piece's last socketed gem.\n631: 5. Press `E` to equip the piece so its stats and gems affect derived stats.\n632: \n633: The UI displays `used sockets / total sockets` for every gear card.\n634: \n635: ## 6. Terminal playtest\n636: \n637: `client/src/playtest/main.cpp` provides a text-oriented executable for quick\n638: manual checks of mining, dungeon, and reward behavior. It links through\n639: `emberdeep_client` and therefore uses the same shared core logic as the\n640: graphical application.\n641: \n642: ## 7. Data files\n643: \n644: The `data/` directory currently contains:\n645: \n646: | File | Purpose |\n647: |---|---|\n648: | `materials.json` | Material definitions and mining values |\n649: | `layers.json` | Layer names, tiers, and unlock requirements |\n650: | `gems.json` | Gem IDs, names, rarity, effects, and priority |\n651: | `bosses.json` | Campaign boss content definitions |\n652: | `perks.json` | Vow/perk definitions |\n653: | `dungeon_zones.json` | Dungeon zone content |\n654: \n655: The current core defaults in `data_model.hpp` and `campaign.cpp` are the\n656: authoritative runtime references used by the tested C++ systems. Data files are\n657: content references and should be synchronized whenever a mechanic is renamed.\n658: In particular, `data/bosses.json` still contains the old Ignathia challenge\n659: name `wyrmfire-run`; the code uses `Heartpierce`.\n660: \n661: ## 8. Server and persistence architecture\n662: \n663: ### 8.1 Local saves\n664: \n665: Guest progress and signed-in progress are stored locally. Account-specific\n666: names are:\n667: \n668: ```text\n669: emberdeep-guest.sav\n670: emberdeep-<username>.sav\n671: ```\n672: \n673: The active account profile is stored in `emberdeep.profile`. Signing out does\n674: not delete saves. The client loads the account's local save before attempting\n675: cloud synchronization.\n676: \n677: ### 8.2 API\n678: \n679: `server/src/api_main.cpp` implements a small HTTP API with:\n680: \n681: - `GET /health` and `GET /healthz`;\n682: - `GET /readyz`;\n683: - `POST /v1/auth/register`;\n684: - `POST /v1/auth/login`;\n685: - authenticated `GET /v1/saves/latest`;\n686: - authenticated `PUT /v1/saves`.\n687: \n688: Save payloads are serialized snapshots encoded as hexadecimal inside a simple\n689: JSON field. Authentication uses bearer tokens. The fallback local backend\n690: stores users and saves under the configured API data root.\n691: \n692: The API validates usernames and minimum password length, hashes local fallback\n693: password material with OpenSSL SHA-256, checks bearer authentication for save\n694: routes, and returns explicit HTTP error responses.\n695: \n696: ### 8.3 PostgreSQL\n697: \n698: With PostgreSQL enabled, the API can use `server/src/postgres_backend.cpp`.\n699: Database schema changes are in:\n700: \n701: - `server/migrations/0001_init.sql`;\n702: - `server/migrations/0002_add_access_token_revoked_at.sql`;\n703: - `server/migrations/0003_extend_existing_access_tokens.sql`.\n704: \n705: PostgreSQL support includes account records, password/token handling, save\n706: revisions, token lifetime, and health/readiness integration.\n707: \n708: ### 8.4 Deployment\n709: \n710: Deployment assets include:\n711: \n712: - `docker-compose.yml`;\n713: - `server/Dockerfile`;\n714: - `deploy/Caddyfile`;\n715: - `deploy/README.md`;\n716: - `.env.example`;\n717: - PostgreSQL initialization and existing-volume upgrade scripts.\n718: \n719: The production profile uses PostgreSQL 16, the C++ API, and Caddy for HTTPS.\n720: The intended commands are:\n721: \n722: ```bash\n723: cp .env.example .env\n724: # Replace every placeholder with strong secrets.\n725: docker compose up -d --build --pull always\n726: curl https://your-domain.example/healthz\n727: curl https://your-domain.example/readyz\n728: docker compose ps\n729: ```\n730: \n731: Authenticated sessions default to a configurable 30-day lifetime. A migration\n732: extends existing active sessions once so a server restart does not invalidate\n733: the desktop client's stored token.\n734: \n735: The deployment validation todo remains blocked only because a Docker daemon was\n736: unavailable in the development environment; local C++ build and test\n737: validation succeeded.\n738: \n739: ## 9.
