Accounts, Save Sync & Autosave\n23: \n24: ### 15.1 Accounts\n25: - **Guest-first:** on first launch the client silently creates a guest account (`POST /auth/guest`) and stores a device secret locally (OS credential store if available, otherwise a user-only-readable file). The player can play immediately.\n26: - **Link account:** email + password (Argon2id via libsodium) and a unique username (3–16 chars, profanity-filtered). Email verification is a later feature. Linking is prompted at Level 10 (skippable) and always available in Settings.\n27: - **Tokens:** access token (JWT, 15 min) + rotating refresh token (30 days, stored hashed, revocable per device).\n28: - **Account deletion:** `DELETE /account` removes the account, saves, and personal data; guild memberships are cleared, leaderboard rows removed.\n29: \n30: ### 15.2 Local Autosave (always on)\n31: Autosave every **10 s** when the state is dirty, on every screen change, on window close, and on minigame completion. Atomic writes, 3 rolling backups (Section 2.4).\n32: \n33: ### 15.3 Cloud Autosave & Sync\n34: - **Push** when logged in and dirty: every **60 s**, immediately after major events (first boss clear, legendary drop, ascension, guild join/leave), on focus loss, and on exit (best effort, 3 s timeout).\n35: - **Pull** on launch (after login) and when \"Sync now\" is pressed.\n36: - **Payload:** `schema_version`, `device_id`, `device_name`, `base_revision`, `progress_score`, `client_saved_at`, `sha256`, and the zstd-compressed JSON save (typically 10–60 KB).\n37: - **Failure handling:** exponential backoff with jitter (5 s → 5 min), only the *latest* state is ever queued, and play is never interrupted.\n38: - **Server-side history:** keep the last 20 revisions plus one per day for 30 days. Players can restore any of them from Settings.\n39: \n40: ### 15.4 Conflict Resolution\n41: The push is conditional on `base_revision`.\n42: 1. Server revision == `base_revision` → accepted, new revision returned.\n43: 2. Server revision > `base_revision` and the **local state is unchanged** since the last sync → silently fast-forward (download cloud save).\n44: 3. Both changed → `409 Conflict` with the cloud save's metadata. The client shows a **Choose Save** dialog with both sides' level, playtime, gold, bosses cleared, ascensions, device name and last-played time, and recommends the one with the higher `progress_score`. The loser is kept in history (server) and as a local backup, so the choice is reversible.\n45: 4. Stretch: automatic merge of *monotonic* fields only (lifetime stats, achievements, best boss stars). Never auto-merge currencies or inventory.\n46: \n47: `progress_score = ascensions×10^9 + echoRank×10^7 + bossesCleared×10^6 + level×10^3 + floor(log2(lifetimeGold+1))`.\n48: \n49: ### 15.5 Sync UI\n50: - Top-bar cloud icon: **Synced ✓ / Syncing… / Offline / Conflict! / Error**.\n51: - Settings → Account: link account, log in, log out, Sync now, Download cloud save, Upload local save, Restore from history, Export/Import file, Delete account.\n52: - Server-issued rewards (season prizes, Colossus rewards) arrive through the **Inbox** and are applied to the local save on claim (then synced normally).\n53: \n54: ---\n55: \n56: ## 16. Guilds\n57: \n58: Unlocked at **player Level 10** and requires an account.\n59: \n60: ### 16.1 Basics\n61: - **Identity:** unique name (3–20 chars), tag (2–4 chars), description, **emblem** built from shapes (base shape + colors + a symbol shape, stored as JSON and drawn procedurally), join mode (**Open / Request / Invite-only**), minimum level.\n62: - **Size:** max **30 members**.\n63: - **Creation:** Level 10 and 5,000 gold. Leaving a guild adds a 12-hour cooldown before joining another.\n64: \n65: | Role | Permissions |\n66: |---|---|\n67: | **Leader** (1) | Everything, transfer leadership, disband, edit settings |\n68: | **Officer** | Accept/reject applications, invite, kick members, start Guild Projects, pin chat messages |\n69: | **Member** | Donate, fight the Colossus, chat, leave |\n70: \n71: If the Leader is inactive for **14 days**, leadership auto-transfers to the most senior active Officer (or member).\n72: \n73: ### 16.2 Guild Progression\n74: Guild XP comes from donations, Colossus damage and member Arena wins. Guild level (1–20) unlocks **economy and mining perks only**. Guild perks never apply to Arena stats, so PvP stays fair.\n75: \n76: | Guild Lv | Perk (cumulative) |\n77: |---|---|\n78: | 2 | +2% XP |\n79: | 4 | +2% gold from sales and the dungeon |\n80: | 6 | +1 Gem Hollow swing |\n81: | 8 | +3 Luck |\n82: | 10 | +5% Mining Power |\n83: | 12 | +1 Colossus session per day |\n84: | 15 | +5% Arcane Dust chance; unlock emblem frames |\n85: | 20 | +10% Mining Power total; golden guild banner |\n86: \n87: ### 16.3 Donations & Projects\n88: - Members donate gold, ingots or Arcane Dust to the **Treasury** (daily caps by player level to stop pure pay-to-rank style hoarding). Donating grants personal **Guild Coins** and guild XP.\n89: - Officers start a **Guild Project** to raise the guild level. Cost for level L: `gold = 10,000 × 1.7^(L−1)`, `ingots = 50 × L` of tier `ceil(L/2)`.\n90: - **Guild Coins shop:** Arcane Dust bundles, cosmetic emblem frames, pickaxe trail effects (particle presets), name-plate borders.\n91: \n92: ### 16.4 The Sleeping Colossus (Weekly Co-op Boss)\n93: - A giant block with a **shared HP pool**, reset every Monday 00:00 UTC. Colossus level rises by 1 each week it is defeated, and drops by 1 if it is not (min 1).\n94: - **Sessions:** each member gets **3 sessions per day** (+ guild perks). A session is a **30-second mining frenzy** on the Colossus using the player's normal click-damage formula (hardness ignored), hit rate capped at 12 hits/s.\n95: - **HP target:** computed at week start from members' recorded power: `maxHp = 0.7 × Σ(expected weekly damage per member)` so it is beatable if about 70% of the guild participates.\n96: - **Server validation:** the client submits `{clicks, damage, durationMs}`; the server clamps damage to `profile power × critAvg × min(clicks, 12/s × duration)` and adds it to the pool.\n97: - **Rewards:** on defeat, everyone with ≥ 1 session receives baseline rewards; the top 3 contributors get extras. Rewards arrive in the **Inbox** (Boss Essence, Arcane Dust, gems, Guild Coins). System lines for joins, leaves, promotions and Colossus events.\n101: - Server-side profanity filter, per-user mute, and \"Report\" on any message. Muted accounts (moderator action) cannot post. No real-time netcode needed, and results cannot be forged.\n117: \n118: ### 17.2 Arena Build (Snapshot)\n119: - Contains: level, equipped gear (template, upgrade level, gems, enchant, awakening), Vows, and legendary IDs. {"step_index":24,"source":"SYSTEM","type":"SYSTEM_MESSAGE","status":"DONE","created_at":"2026-10-08T10:42:13Z","content":"The following is a <SYSTEM_MESSAGE> not actually sent by the user. It is provided by the system as important information to pay attention to.\n\n<SYSTEM_MESSAGE>\n[Message] timestamp=2026-10-08T10:42:13Z sender=e4b11f9a-68fb-4551-8a8b-2328b9264751 priority=MESSAGE_PRIORITY_HIGH content=## Security Audit Report — Emberdeep Client & Core\n\n### 1. **CRITICAL — Plaintext Auth Token Stored on Disk**\n- **File:** `core/src/cloud.cpp:124-133`, `core/include/ed/cloud.hpp:17-21`\n- **Description:** `writeAccountProfile()` writes the auth token in plaintext to a file (`emberdeep.profile`): `\"token=\" + profile.token`. Anyone with read access to the filesystem can steal the session token. No file permission restrictions are set on the output file.\n- **Fix:** Store tokens using OS keychain (macOS Keychain, Windows Credential Store, libsecret on Linux). At minimum, set restrictive file permissions (`chmod 0600`) on the profile file after writing it.\n\n### 2. **CRITICAL — No TLS; All Network Traffic Sent in Cleartext**\n- **File:** `core/src/cloud.cpp:46-95`\n- **Description:** The `request()` function opens a raw TCP socket and sends HTTP (not HTTPS). Credentials (username, password in JSON body at lines 137-144) and auth tokens (Bearer header at line 73) are transmitted in plaintext. Any network observer can intercept them.\n- **Fix:** Use TLS (e.g., OpenSSL, libcurl with HTTPS, or OS-native TLS). Validate server certificates. Never send credentials over plain HTTP.\n\n### 3. **HIGH — JSON Injection in Login/Register Requests**\n- **File:** `core/src/cloud.cpp:135-144`\n- **Description:** `cloudRegister()` and `cloudLogin()` construct JSON via string concatenation: `\"{\\\"username\\\":\\\"\" + username + \"\\\",\\\"password\\\":\\\"\" + password + \"\\\"}\"`. If the username or password contains a double-quote or backslash character, the resulting JSON is malformed or an attacker can inject arbitrary JSON fields. While main.cpp limits input to printable ASCII (line 289), the core API is public and can be called with any string.\n- **Fix:** Properly escape JSON strings (at minimum escape `\"` and `\\`), or use a JSON library.\n\n### 4. **HIGH — Save File Manipulation / Arbitrary Value Injection**\n- **File:** `core/src/save.cpp:51-129`, `core/src/game_state.cpp:99-200`\n- **Description:** The save format is a simple `key=value` text file with no integrity check (no HMAC, checksum, or signature). A user can trivially edit `emberdeep-*.sav` to set `gold=999999999`, `level=60`, `pickaxe_tier=10`, etc. Deserialized integer values have no range validation — negative gold, negative levels, or absurdly high values are all accepted. If this is a competitive/online game with cloud saves, crafted saves can be uploaded.\n- **Specific lines:**\n  - `game_state.cpp:134-136`: gold parsed with no range check\n  - `game_state.cpp:127-129`: level parsed with no cap enforcement\n  - `save.cpp:81-84`: inventory counts parsed with no upper-bound check\n  - `save.cpp:109-114`: campaign vectors resized to arbitrary index from save file (memory exhaustion DoS possible by setting `campaign.999999999=...`)\n- **Fix:** Add HMAC-based integrity verification to save files. Validate all deserialized values against business-logic ranges (e.g., level 1–60, gold ≥ 0, tier 1–10). Cap the campaign index to prevent unbounded vector allocation.\n\n### 5. **HIGH — Unbounded Campaign Vector Allocation (DoS)**\n- **File:** `core/src/save.cpp:107-120`\n- **Description:** When deserializing campaign data, the key `campaign.<index>` causes the vectors to be resized to `index + 1`. A crafted save file with `campaign.2147483647=1,3` would attempt to allocate a multi-gigabyte vector, causing OOM crash.\n- **Fix:** Enforce a maximum campaign size (e.g., reject indices beyond the number of bosses, currently 10-11).\n\n### 6. **HIGH — HTTP Response Parsing Crash (std::stoi on Malformed Data)**\n- **File:** `core/src/cloud.cpp:89-91`\n- **Description:** `std::stoi(response.substr(statusEnd + 1, 3))` is called on the HTTP status line. If the backend (or a MITM attacker) returns a malformed response where the status position contains non-numeric characters, `std::stoi` throws `std::invalid_argument` which is not caught, crashing the application.\n- **Fix:** Wrap in try/catch or use a safe parsing function (like the `parseIntValue` used in game_state.cpp).\n\n### 7. **MEDIUM — Auth Credentials Sent to Hardcoded Unverified Host**\n- **File:** `client/src/app/main.cpp:83-84`, `core/src/cloud.cpp:135-148`\n- **Description:** The backend host defaults to `127.0.0.1:8080`. There is no mechanism to verify the server's identity (no TLS certificate pinning, no TLS at all). If the host were configurable or DNS-resolved, credentials would be sent to whatever responds on that address.\n- **Fix:** Implement TLS with certificate validation. If the host is ever made configurable, validate it rigorously.\n\n### 8. **MEDIUM — Race Condition: Save Worker Accesses Shared State Without Full Synchronization**\n- **File:** `client/src/app/main.cpp:181-201`\n- **Description:** The save worker thread (line 181) captures `app` by reference. While `pendingSave` is protected by the mutex, line 195 reads `app.account.mode` and `app.account.token` and line 197 writes `app.notice` — all without holding the lock. These are data races (UB in C++). The `notice` string could be corrupted if the main thread reads it simultaneously.\n- **Fix:** Copy `account.mode`, `account.token`, and `backendHost`/`backendPort` into the snapshot under the lock. Avoid writing `app.notice` from the worker thread; use a thread-safe queue or atomic flag instead.\n\n### 9. **MEDIUM — Save File Path Not Sanitized Against Path Traversal**\n- **File:** `client/src/app/main.cpp:131-148`\n- **Description:** `safeAccountId()` replaces most special chars but the result is concatenated into a relative path: `\"emberdeep-\" + safeAccountId(accountId) + \".sav\"`. Since `safeAccountId` allows `-` and `_`, an account ID like `../../../etc/cron.d/malicious` would become `.._.._.._.._etc_cron.d_malicious` which is safe. **However**, the `savePath` is a relative path — it resolves relative to CWD, which could be unexpected. Also `profilePath` is hardcoded as `\"emberdeep.profile\"` (line 75) and similarly relative.\n- **Fix:** Resolve save and profile paths to an explicit application data directory using an absolute path. Validate that the final path is within the expected directory.\n\n### 10. **MEDIUM — Naive JSON Field Parser Vulnerable to Injection**\n- **File:** `core/src/cloud.cpp:97-104`\n- **Description:** The `field()` function does minimal JSON parsing — it searches for `\"key\":\"` and reads until the next `\"`. It does not handle escaped quotes (`\\\"`), so a server response containing `\"token\":\"real_token\\\",\\\"injected\\\":\\\"data\"` would return truncated/wrong values. This could lead to auth token truncation or misinterpretation.\n- **Fix:** Use a proper JSON parser, or at minimum handle `\\\"` escape sequences.\n\n### 11. **MEDIUM — Cloud Save Deserialization Trusts Server Completely**\n- **File:** `core/src/cloud.cpp:157-162`, `client/src/app/main.cpp:204-219`\n- **Description:** `decodeCloudSnapshot()` hex-decodes data from the server response and deserializes it directly into game state with no validation. A compromised or malicious server could send crafted save data that exploits the deserialization vulnerabilities (issue #4 and #5).\n- **Fix:** Apply the same validation/range checks to cloud-loaded data as to local saves. Consider signing save data client-side before upload and verifying on download.\n\n### 12. **MEDIUM — Password Stored in Memory as Plaintext String (No Secure Erasure)**\n- **File:** `client/src/app/main.cpp:82, 306, 309, 499-500`\n- **Description:** `authPassword` is a `std::string`. After authentication, `authPassword.clear()` is called (line 500), but `std::string::clear()` does not overwrite the memory — the password remains in the heap until the allocator reuses that memory. A memory dump or core dump would reveal it.\n- **Fix:** Use `SecureZeroMemory` / `explicit_bzero` on the string's buffer before clearing. Consider using a custom allocator that zero-fills on deallocation.\n\n### 13. **LOW — Account Profile Key Injection**\n- **File:** `core/src/cloud.cpp:108-121, 124-133`\n- **Description:** `writeAccountProfile()` writes `username=<value>` without escaping newlines or `=` in the value. If a username contained a newline followed by `token=stolen`, `readAccountProfile()` would parse it as a separate key-value pair and overwrite the token. The input validation in main.cpp (printable ASCII only, lines 289-291) mitigates this since newlines are excluded, but the core API is not self-protecting.\n- **Fix:** Escape or reject newlines and `=` characters in profile values at the serialization layer.\n\n### 14. **LOW — No Save File Version Validation**\n- **File:** `core/src/save.cpp:51-66`\n- **Description:** The `emberdeep_save_version=1` line is written (line 21) but never validated on read. A save from an incompatible future version would be silently loaded, potentially misinterpreting fields.\n- **Fix:** Check the version on read and reject or migrate incompatible versions.\n\n### 15. **LOW — Gear Deserialization Has No Field Count/Content Validation**\n- **File:** `core/src/save.cpp:85-106`\n- **Description:** Gear fields like `slot`, `itemId`, `name` are read from the save file without any validation. Arbitrary strings can be injected as slot names, item IDs, or gear names, which could cause unexpected behavior if the rest of the code assumes these come from a known set.\n- **Fix:** Validate gear field values against known enumerations (valid slot names, valid item ID patterns).\n\n### 16. **LOW — Information Leakage in Error Messages**\n- **File:** `client/src/app/main.cpp:168, 333`\n- **Description:** Cloud sync error messages (line 168: `\"cloud sync failed: \" + result.error`) display the raw server error response to the user. This could leak internal server details (stack traces, DB errors) to the player.\n- **Fix:** Display generic user-facing error messages; log detailed errors to a file only.\n\n### Summary Table\n\n| # | Severity | Category | File | Lines |\n|---|----------|----------|------|-------|\n| 1 | **Critical** | Credential storage | cloud.cpp | 124-133 |\n| 2 | **Critical** | Missing TLS | cloud.cpp | 46-95 |\n| 3 | **High** | Injection | cloud.cpp | 135-144 |\n| 4 | **High** | Save manipulation | save.cpp, game_state.cpp | 51-129, 99-200 |\n| 5 | **High** | DoS | save.cpp | 107-120 |\n| 6 | **High** | Crash | cloud.cpp | 89-91 |\n| 7 | **Medium** | Auth security | main.cpp, cloud.cpp | 83-84 |\n| 8 | **Medium** | Data race | main.cpp | 181-201 |\n| 9 | **Medium** | Path handling | main.cpp | 131-148 |\n| 10 | **Medium** | Parser flaw | cloud.cpp | 97-104 |\n| 11 | **Medium** | Trust boundary | cloud.cpp, main.cpp | 157-162, 204-219 |\n| 12 | **Medium** | Memory safety | main.cpp | 82, 499-500 |\n| 13 | **Low** | Injection | cloud.cpp | 108-133 |\n| 14 | **Low** | Version check | save.cpp | 51-66 |\n| 15 | **Low** | Validation | save.cpp | 85-106 |\n| 16 | **Low** | Info leakage | main.cpp | 168, 333 |\n\n</SYSTEM_MESSAGE>"}

{"step_index":32,"source":"SYSTEM","type":"SYSTEM_MESSAGE","status":"DONE","created_at":"2026-10-08T10:42:31Z","content":"The following is a <SYSTEM_MESSAGE> not actually sent by the user. It is provided by the system as important information to pay attention to.\n\n<SYSTEM_MESSAGE>\n[Message] timestamp=2026-10-08T10:42:28Z sender=3ff51d09-41a2-4f6b-9a2d-5136566e82ed priority=MESSAGE_PRIORITY_HIGH content=## Emberdeep Server Security Audit — Full Findings\n\nI read every line of the 12 requested files. Below are all vulnerabilities found, ordered by severity.\n\n---\n\n### 🔴 CRITICAL\n\n#### 1. Deterministic, non-secret session tokens (file-based auth path)\n**File:** `server/src/api_main.cpp`, lines 203–205\n**Description:** In the flat-file user backend, the salt is `digest(username + \"-emberdeep-salt\")` (deterministic, no randomness), and the session token is `digest(username + passwordHash + \"session\")` — also completely deterministic. This means:\n- The token **never changes** across logins — it is permanent and cannot be rotated or revoked.\n- An attacker who knows a user's password hash (e.g., from the `users.db` file) can reconstruct the token offline.\n- Two users with the same password on different deployments using the same username will have identical tokens.\n\n**Fix:** Generate a cryptographically random token on every login (like the Postgres path does with `RAND_bytes`). Store only the token hash; return the raw token to the client.\n\n#### 2. Weak password hashing — plain SHA-256 (file-based auth path)\n**File:** `server/src/api_main.cpp`, lines 35–42, 204\n**Description:** Passwords are hashed with a single SHA-256 round (`digest(salt + password)`). SHA-256 is not a password-hashing function — it has no work factor and can be brute-forced at billions of hashes/second on a GPU. The Postgres path correctly uses `crypt(... gen_salt('bf',12))` (bcrypt), but the flat-file path is critically weak.\n**Fix:** Use bcrypt, scrypt, or Argon2id for password hashing in the file-based path. If this path is only for development, at minimum document that and ensure it cannot be used in production.\n\n#### 3. Plaintext credential storage in `users.db`\n**File:** `server/src/api_main.cpp`, lines 256–262\n**Description:** Tokens and password hashes are stored in a pipe-delimited flat file (`users.db`) with no file-system permission management. The tokens are stored in cleartext (not hashed), so anyone who reads the file gets immediate access to all accounts.\n**Fix:** Store only the hash of the token. Set restrictive file permissions (0600).\n\n---\n\n### 🔴 HIGH\n\n#### 4. Path traversal in `SaveBackend` via `accountId`\n**File:** `server/src/save_backend.cpp`, lines 9, 13\n**Description:** The `accountId` (which is the `username` from the flat-file path, or the username from Postgres) is directly concatenated into a filesystem path: `root_ / (accountId + \".sav\")`. In the flat-file auth path, the username is validated with an allow-list (`alphanumeric + _-`), which mitigates this. However, the `SaveBackend` interface itself does **no** validation, so any future caller or code path that passes an unvalidated string (e.g., containing `../`) could read/write arbitrary files.\n**Fix:** Add a defensive check inside `SaveBackend::save()` and `SaveBackend::load()` that rejects any `accountId` containing `/`, `\\`, or `..`. Defense in depth.\n\n#### 5. Unbounded request body — DoS via memory exhaustion\n**File:** `server/src/api_main.cpp`, lines 265–294\n**Description:** The HTTP server reads headers and body without any size limit. The `Content-Length` header is trusted and the server will `recv()` until `body.size() >= contentLength`. An attacker can send `Content-Length: 999999999999` and the server will attempt to allocate that much memory. There is an `EMBERDEEP_MAX_BODY_BYTES` env var in `docker-compose.yml` (line 31) but **it is never read or enforced in the code**.\n**Fix:** Read and enforce `EMBERDEEP_MAX_BODY_BYTES` in the `serve()` function. Reject requests exceeding the limit with a 413 response.\n\n#### 6. Single-threaded blocking server — DoS via connection starvation\n**File:** `server/src/api_main.cpp`, lines 339–342\n**Description:** The `accept()` loop handles one connection at a time synchronously (`serve(api, client)` blocks). A single slow client (e.g., one that connects but never sends data) blocks the entire server. There is no read timeout on `recv()`.\n**Fix:** Add `SO_RCVTIMEO` on client sockets. Consider using a thread pool or async I/O. At minimum, set a timeout so slow-loris attacks can't hold the server indefinitely.\n\n#### 7. No rate limiting on authentication endpoints\n**File:** `server/src/api_main.cpp`, lines 94–97\n**Description:** `/v1/auth/register` and `/v1/auth/login` have no rate limiting, allowing unlimited brute-force password guessing and mass account creation. Caddy (the reverse proxy) also doesn't add rate limiting.\n**Fix:** Add rate limiting at the Caddy layer (e.g., `caddy-ratelimit` plugin) or in the application. Consider exponential backoff or account lockout after N failed attempts.\n\n#### 8. Race condition in save revision numbering (Postgres path)\n**File:** `server/src/postgres_backend.cpp`, lines 167–169\n**Description:** The revision number for a new save is computed via a subquery `COALESCE((SELECT max(revision)+1 FROM saves WHERE account_id=a.id),1)` inside an INSERT. Two concurrent saves for the same account can both read the same `max(revision)` and insert with the same revision number, violating the `PRIMARY KEY (account_id, revision)` constraint and causing one to fail silently.\n**Fix:** Use a `SERIALIZABLE` transaction, or an advisory lock, or a `SEQUENCE` per account, or use `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT` with retry logic.\n\n#### 9. Single shared PGconn without thread safety\n**File:** `server/src/postgres_backend.cpp`, lines 50–52; `api_main.cpp`, lines 139\n**Description:** There is a single `PGconn*` shared across all requests. `PQexecParams` is not thread-safe when called concurrently on the same connection. Although the current server is single-threaded, if multi-threading is ever added (per fix #6), this will cause data corruption. Even now, the `mutex_` in `Api` only protects the flat-file user map, not the Postgres calls.\n**Fix:** Use a connection pool (e.g., one `PGconn` per request or use `libpqxx` with pooling). At minimum, add a mutex around all `PGconn` usage.\n\n---\n\n### 🟡 MEDIUM\n\n#### 10. Constant-value salt defeats the purpose of salting (file-based path)\n**File:** `server/src/api_main.cpp`, line 203\n**Description:** The salt is `digest(username + \"-emberdeep-salt\")` — it's fully deterministic from the username, not random. This means identical passwords for different users with predictable usernames produce predictable hashes. Combined with the SHA-256 issue, this enables pre-computation attacks across all users.\n**Fix:** Generate a random salt per user (e.g., 16 random bytes).\n\n#### 11. No token expiry or revocation (file-based auth path)\n**File:** `server/src/api_main.cpp`, lines 205, 230–239\n**Description:** Tokens in the flat-file path never expire and cannot be revoked. Once issued, a token is valid forever. There is no logout endpoint. An attacker who obtains a token has permanent access.\n**Fix:** Add an expiration timestamp and a revocation mechanism for file-based tokens. Provide a logout endpoint.\n\n#### 12. No CORS headers\n**File:** `deploy/Caddyfile`, lines 1–10; `server/src/api_main.cpp`, lines 300–303\n**Description:** No CORS headers are set at either the Caddy or application level. If the game client ever runs in a browser, this will either break (browser blocks requests) or, if `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` is added hastily later, could expose the API to cross-origin attacks.\n**Fix:** Configure explicit CORS headers (at the Caddy level) with an allowed origin list appropriate for the game client.\n\n#### 13. `Content-Length` parsing trusts the first match, no overflow protection\n**File:** `server/src/api_main.cpp`, lines 286–289\n**Description:** `std::stoul` is used to parse `Content-Length` without any bounds checking. An extremely large value could cause `std::out_of_range` exception (uncaught, crashes the server) or lead to the unbounded allocation described in #5. A negative or malformed value could also cause issues.\n**Fix:** Wrap the parse in a try-catch, validate the result against a maximum, and return 413 if exceeded.\n\n#### 14. Naive JSON parsing vulnerable to injection\n**File:** `server/src/api_main.cpp`, lines 44–55\n**Description:** `jsonField()` does naive string search for `\"key\":\"`. It doesn't handle escape sequences (e.g., `\\\"`) inside JSON values, so a crafted input could extract the wrong value or include unexpected data. `json()` (line 53–55) directly interpolates values into a JSON string without escaping, so a value containing `\"` breaks the JSON structure and could be used for injection if the response is parsed by a client.\n**Fix:** Use a proper JSON parser (e.g., nlohmann/json, RapidJSON). At minimum, escape `\"` and `\\` in `json()` output and handle escape sequences in `jsonField()`.\n\n#### 15. Missing `Content-Security-Policy` header\n**File:** `deploy/Caddyfile`, lines 4–9\n**Description:** While several good security headers are present (HSTS, X-Content-Type-Options, X-Frame-Options, Referrer-Policy), `Content-Security-Policy` is missing. For a JSON API this is low risk, but it's still a hardening gap.\n**Fix:** Add `Content-Security-Policy \"default-src 'none'\"` to the header block.\n\n#### 16. Username enumeration via distinct error responses\n**File:** `server/src/api_main.cpp`, line 202; `server/src/postgres_backend.cpp`, lines 89–94\n**Description:** Registration returns `409 \"username already exists\"` which confirms that a username is taken. This is a minor information disclosure that aids targeted attacks.\n**Fix:** Generally acceptable for usability, but consider returning a generic \"registration failed\" if this is a concern.\n\n---\n\n### 🟢 LOW\n\n#### 17. No maximum username/password length\n**File:** `server/src/api_main.cpp`, lines 187–189\n**Description:** Username validation checks minimum length (3) and character set, but there's no maximum length. An extremely long username or password could cause excessive hashing time (especially in the bcrypt Postgres path where a 10MB password would be expensive) or large flat-file entries.\n**Fix:** Enforce a reasonable maximum (e.g., username ≤ 32 chars, password ≤ 128 chars).\n\n#### 18. `EMBERDEEP_TOKEN_SECRET` env var is defined but never used\n**File:** `.env.example`, line 6; `docker-compose.yml`, line 29\n**Description:** `EMBERDEEP_TOKEN_SECRET` is passed to the API container and required (`?set`), but no code ever reads it. This suggests intended HMAC-based token signing that was never implemented, leaving token security weaker than intended.\n**Fix:** Implement HMAC-signed tokens using this secret, or remove the env var to avoid false confidence.\n\n#### 19. Header search is case-sensitive\n**File:** `server/src/api_main.cpp`, lines 171–177\n**Description:** The `header()` function does an exact case-sensitive search for header names. HTTP headers are case-insensitive per RFC 7230. A client sending `authorization: Bearer ...` (lowercase) won't be authenticated.\n**Fix:** Normalize header names to lowercase before searching, or use a case-insensitive find.\n\n#### 20. No graceful shutdown\n**File:** `server/src/api_main.cpp`, lines 339–342\n**Description:** The `while (true)` accept loop has no signal handling. SIGTERM from Docker will kill the process mid-request, potentially corrupting the `users.db` flat file (if a write was in progress via `saveUsers()`).\n**Fix:** Add a signal handler for SIGTERM/SIGINT that sets a flag to break the accept loop, then cleanly finishes in-flight requests.\n\n#### 21. `listen()` backlog of 16 is very small\n**File:** `server/src/api_main.cpp`, line 333\n**Description:** `listen(server, 16)` sets the TCP backlog to 16. Combined with the single-threaded server, this means connections will be dropped under even moderate load.\n**Fix:** Increase the backlog (e.g., 128 or `SOMAXCONN`) and add concurrent connection handling.\n\n#### 22. Postgres connection string may appear in error output\n**File:** `server/src/postgres_backend.cpp`, line 56\n**Description:** If the connection fails, libpq's error message (accessed via `PQerrorMessage`) may contain the connection string including the password. While it's not explicitly logged in this code, the connection string is stored in memory and could appear in core dumps.\n**Fix:** Consider using `PQconnectdbParams` with separate parameters, or scrub errors before logging.\n\n---\n\n### Summary by Severity\n\n| Severity | Count |\n|----------|-------|\n| Critical | 3 |\n| High | 6 |\n| Medium | 7 |\n| Low | 6 |\n| **Total** | **22** |\n\n**Most urgent items:** The flat-file auth path (#1, #2, #3) has critically weak cryptography and should either be hardened or disabled in production. The unbounded request body (#5) and single-threaded DoS (#6) are the highest-priority operational risks. The race condition (#8) will cause data loss under concurrent use.\n\n</SYSTEM_MESSAGE>"}

Unbounded request body — DoS via memory exhaustion\n- **File:** [api_main.cpp](file:///Users/kacperdurak/desktop/game/server/src/api_main.cpp) ~L265–294\n- `Content-Length` trusted without limit. `EMBERDEEP_MAX_BODY_BYTES` is in docker-compose but **never read or enforced in code**.\n- **Fix:** Read and enforce the env var. Race condition in save revision numbering (Postgres)\n- **File:** [postgres_backend.cpp](file:///Users/kacperdurak/desktop/game/server/src/postgres_backend.cpp) ~L167–169\n- Two concurrent saves can read the same `max(revision)` → duplicate PK → silent failure.\n- **Fix:** Use `SERIALIZABLE` isolation, advisory lock, or `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT` with retry.\n\n---\n\n## 🟡 Medium (12)\n\n### M1. Race condition — save worker reads shared state outside lock\n- **File:** [main.cpp](file:///Users/kacperdurak/desktop/game/client/src/app/main.cpp) ~L181–201\n- Worker thread reads `app.account.mode`, `app.account.token`, writes `app.notice` without holding the mutex → data race (UB).\n- **Fix:** Copy shared fields into the snapshot under the lock. Use a thread-safe queue for notices.\n\n### M2. Naive JSON parser in cloud client\n- **File:** [cloud.cpp](file:///Users/kacperdurak/desktop/game/core/src/cloud.cpp) ~L97–104\n- `field()` doesn't handle `\\\"` escape sequences → truncated/wrong token extraction from server responses.\n- **Fix:** Use a proper JSON parser.\n\n### M3. Naive JSON parser in server\n- **File:** [api_main.cpp](file:///Users/kacperdurak/desktop/game/server/src/api_main.cpp) ~L44–55\n- Same issue server-side. `json()` outputs unescaped values → response injection.\n- **Fix:** Use nlohmann/json (already a vcpkg dependency in the spec).\n\n### M4. Cloud save deserialization trusts server completely\n- **File:** [cloud.cpp](file:///Users/kacperdurak/desktop/game/core/src/cloud.cpp) ~L157–162\n- Hex-decoded server data deserialized directly into game state. A compromised server can exploit all save-parsing vulnerabilities (H2, H3).\n- **Fix:** Apply the same validation to cloud-loaded data.\n\n### M5. Save file path resolved relative to CWD\n- **File:** [main.cpp](file:///Users/kacperdurak/desktop/game/client/src/app/main.cpp) ~L131–148\n- Save and profile paths are relative (`emberdeep-<user>.sav`). Resolve to an explicit app data directory.\n\n### M6. Password stays in heap after `clear()`\n- **File:** [main.cpp](file:///Users/kacperdurak/desktop/game/client/src/app/main.cpp) ~L82, 499–500\n- `std::string::clear()` doesn't zero memory. A core dump reveals the password.\n- **Fix:** `explicit_bzero` on the buffer before clearing.\n\n### M7. No token expiry or revocation (flat-file auth)\n- **File:** [api_main.cpp](file:///Users/kacperdurak/desktop/game/server/src/api_main.cpp) ~L205, 230–239\n- Tokens never expire and there's no logout endpoint. Stolen token = permanent access.\n\n### M8. `Content-Length` parsing crash\n- **File:** [api_main.cpp](file:///Users/kacperdurak/desktop/game/server/src/api_main.cpp) ~L286–289\n- `std::stoul` without try/catch. Malformed or overflow value crashes the server.\n- **Fix:** Wrap in try/catch, validate against maximum.\n\n### M9. Hardcoded unverified backend host\n- **File:** [main.cpp](file:///Users/kacperdurak/desktop/game/client/src/app/main.cpp) ~L83–84\n- Default `127.0.0.1:8080` with no server identity verification.\n\n### M10. Single PGconn without thread safety\n- **File:** [postgres_backend.cpp](file:///Users/kacperdurak/desktop/game/server/src/postgres_backend.cpp) ~L50–52\n- Single shared connection. `PQexecParams` is not thread-safe. Will corrupt data if multi-threading is added.\n- **Fix:** Connection pool or mutex around all PG calls.\n\n### M11. Username enumeration via distinct error responses\n- **File:** [api_main.cpp](file:///Users/kacperdurak/desktop/game/server/src/api_main.cpp) ~L202\n- Registration returns `409 \"username already exists\"`, confirming existence.\n\n### M12. On break, a roll: **45% gem**, **35% Arcane Dust (2–6)**, **20% gold pouch**.\r\n- **Weak-point ring:** on some veins a ring shrinks; tapping while it is inside the target zone breaks the vein instantly and adds a **+1 gem** bonus.\r\n- **Luck** shifts the gem rarity weights upward by up to +25% of the weight of the tiers above Common.\r\n- **Expected result:** ~5–8 gems, occasionally a Legendary. Show a results summary with rarities highlighted.\r\n- **Rules:** progress is saved mid-event (if the user leaves, they can resume the same day); once finished or expired at midnight, it locks until the next day.\r\n\r\n---\r\n\r\n## 7. Donating grants personal **Guild Coins** and guild XP.\r\n- Officers start a **Guild Project** to raise the guild level. Cost for level L: `gold = 10,000 × 1.7^(L−1)`, `ingots = 50 × L` of tier `ceil(L/2)`.\r\n- **Guild Coins shop:** Arcane Dust bundles, cosmetic emblem frames, pickaxe trail effects (particle presets), name-plate borders.\r\n\r\n### 16.4 The Sleeping Colossus (Weekly Co-op Boss)\r\n- A giant block with a **shared HP pool**, reset every Monday 00:00 UTC. Colossus level rises by 1 each week it is defeated, and drops by 1 if it is not (min 1).\r\n- **Sessions:** each member gets **3 sessions per day** (+ guild perks). A session is a **30-second mining frenzy** on the Colossus using the player's normal click-damage formula (hardness ignored), hit rate capped at 12 hits/s.\r\n- **HP target:** computed at week start from members' recorded power: `maxHp = 0.7 × Σ(expected weekly damage per member)` so it is beatable if about 70% of the guild participates.\r\n- **Server validation:** the client submits `{clicks, damage, durationMs}`; the server clamps damage to `profile power × critAvg × min(clicks, 12/s × duration)` and adds it to the pool.\r\n- **Rewards:** on defeat, everyone with ≥ 1 session receives baseline rewards; the top 3 contributors get extras. Rewards arrive in the **Inbox** (Boss Essence, Arcane Dust, gems, Guild Coins). If not defeated, rewards scale down proportionally to HP dealt.\r\n\r\n### 16.5 Guild Chat\r\n- WebSocket channel `guild:{id}`, 200-char messages, **1 message/second** per user, last 200 messages stored. Docker & Deployment\r\n\r\n**Is Docker a good idea?** *Yes for the server side:* the C++ toolchain, vcpkg dependencies, PostgreSQL and the reverse proxy become reproducible with one command, the same images run on a home server or VPS, and backups/migrations are easy to script. *No for the game client:* it is a native GUI + audio app, so build it natively on CI runners for each OS (an optional Linux build container is fine for CI only).\r\n\r\n### 20.1 Files\r\n```\r\ndocker/\r\n  server.Dockerfile              # multi-stage build\r\n  docker-compose.yml             # production-like stack\r\n  docker-compose.dev.yml         # dev overrides (exposed ports, Adminer, debug logs)\r\n  caddy/Caddyfile\r\n\n"}
